Twisted Elliptic Genera Preprint: KIAS-Q22012
Kimyeong Lee, Kaiwen Sun, Xin Wang

Abstract
We study the twisted elliptic genera of 2d ( 0 , 4 ) (0,4) SCFTs associated with the BPS strings in the twisted circle compactification of 6d rank-one ( 1 , 0 ) (1,0) SCFTs. Such objects can arise when the 6d gauge algebra allows outer automorphism, thus are classified by twisted affine Lie algebras. We study several fascinating aspects of the twisted elliptic genera including 2d localization, twisted elliptic blowup equations, Higgsing and spectral flow symmetry. We derive a recursion formula with respect to the number of strings to exactly compute the twisted elliptic genera. We also investigate the modular bootstrap of twisted one-string elliptic genera and find the modularity of congruence subgroups Γ 1 ​ ( N ) \Gamma_{1}(N) naturally appears with possible N = 2 , 3 , 4 N=2,3,4 . Geometrically, our study solves the refined BPS partition functions of the underlying genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds with N N -section.



1
Introduction

The classification of superconformal field theories (SCFTs) and supersymmetric gauge theories in various dimensions has been a major subject in the study of supersymmetry in the recent decades. Given Nahm’s classification on superconformal algebras
Nahm:1977tg
, the highest dimension in which a nontrivial SCFT can exist is six. It is now well-known that the 6d
(2,0)
SCFTs admit an ADE classification
Witten:1995zh
;
Strominger:1995ac
, while the 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs admit an “atomic classification” with certain generalized quiver structures
Heckman:2015bfa
. See an excellent review
Heckman:2018jxk
. More recently, much progress has been made in the understanding of the compactification of 6d SCFTs to lower dimensions. In particular, the compactification to 5d Kaluza-Klein (KK) theories is mostly understood and results in a conjectural classification on 5d SCFTs by decoupling 5d hypermultiplets from the KK theories
Jefferson:2017ahm
;
Jefferson:2018irk
;
Bhardwaj:2018yhy
;
Bhardwaj:2018vuu
;
Bhardwaj:2019fzv
.

Besides the straightforward circle compactification, it was observed in
Bhardwaj:2019fzv
that when a 6d SCFT has a discrete global symmetry, one can do
twisted circle compactification
to a new 5d KK theory. This process brings in a large class of highly nontrivial 5d KK theories, many of which have fascinating 5d Lagrangian descriptions
Bhardwaj:2020gyu
. There exist two possibilities for the 6d discrete global symmetry: the first kind comes when the gauge algebra allows outer automorphism, in which case the twisted circle compactification means to fold the 6d vector multiplets; the second kind comes when the quiver structure has a discrete symmetry, in which case one folds the 6d tensor multiplets. In the current work, we are interested in the first kind of twisted circle compactification of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs as it is classified by
twisted affine Lie algebras
and related to the modularity of congruence subgroups
\Gamma_{1}(N)
. The second kind of twisted circle compactification for 6d
(2,0)
SCFTs has been studied in
Duan:2021ges
.

The 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs can be geometrically engineered by F-theory compactified on non-compact elliptic Calabi-Yau threefolds. More precisely, such Calabi-Yau geometry
M
is an elliptic fibration over a non-compact base surface
B
in which all curves are simultaneously shrinkable to zero volume. The
\dim H^{1,1}(B,\mathbb{Z})
gives the dimension of the tensor branch, i.e., the rank of the 6d
(1,0)
SCFT. We are particularly interested in the rank one case, where the non-compact base
B
is just
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(-\mathfrak{n})
for
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,3,\dots,8,12
. The geometric engineering suggests that when a 6d
(1,0)
SCFT is put on the 6d Nekrasov Omega background
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\times T^{2}
, the partition function should be equal to the refined topological string partition function on Calabi-Yau
M
. On the other hand, 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs contain the BPS strings or the so-called self-dual strings with worldsheet theory as 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs. From the viewpoint of worldsheet, the elliptic genera of the 2d theories should also be equal to the 6d partition function upon suitable expansion by the number of strings. Along with the circle compactification to 5d KK theory, these yield the well established relation chain

\mathbb{E}^{\textrm{2d }(0,4)\textrm{ SCFT}}=Z_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\times T^{2}}^{\textrm{6d }(1,0)\textrm{ SCFT}}=Z_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}}^{\textrm{5d KK}}=Z^{\textrm{ref. top.}}_{\textrm{non-compact elliptic CY3}}.

(1.1)

In the case of twisted circle compactification, the non-compact elliptic Calabi-Yau threefolds are generalized to non-compact genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds, with additional rational sections or just
N
-sections. The genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau geometries and their role in F-theory compactification have drawn lots of attention in recent years
Morrison:2014era
;
Braun:2014oya
;
Anderson:2018heq
;
Cota:2019cjx
;
Anderson:2019kmx
;
Oehlmann:2019ohh
;
Kimura:2019bzv
;
Knapp:2021vkm
;
Schimannek:2021pau
;
Dierigl:2022zll
. In our situation, the number of sections is naturally reflected by the type of twists:

N=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}2,&A_{2r-1}^{(2)},D_{r}^{(2)},E_{6}^{(2)},\\
3,&D_{4}^{(3)},\\
4,&A_{2r}^{(2)}.\end{array}\right.

(1.2)

The geometric engineering of these special genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds produces the twisted compactification of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs. The BPS strings of the 6d SCFTs on the torus
T^{2}=S^{1}\times S^{1}
also get twisted along one
S^{1}
in the sixth direction such that the elliptic genera now have fractional
q^{1/N}
orders. Although both the 6d SCFTs and the 2d worldsheet theories are in twisted circle compactification, one can still look from 6d and 2d to keep the modularity manifest. Notably, the modularity is no longer the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
of ordinary elliptic genera, but the congruence subgroups
\Gamma_{1}(N)
of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
. In summary, we expect the following twisted version of the relation chain (
1.1
) as

{\mathbb{E}^{\textrm{2d }(0,4)\textrm{ SCFT}}_{\rm twisted}}=Z_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}\times S^{1},\textrm{twisted }}^{\textrm{6d }(1,0)\textrm{ SCFT}}=Z_{\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}}^{\textrm{5d KK}}=Z^{\textrm{ref. top.}}_{\textrm{non-compact genus-one fibered CY3}}.

(1.3)

We are interested in the
twisted elliptic genera
arising here. It should be noted that this term actually has already been used in a different context for 2d
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
SCFTs such as the twisted elliptic genus of K3 surface, see e.g.
Kawai:2009ci
;
Eguchi:2010fg
. The twist in our term comes from the same twist as in twisted affine Lie algebras and is specifically for 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,4)
SCFTs, thus should not be confused. For the second kind of twist, one can also study the associated twisted elliptic genera, which for the 6d
(2,0)
cases have been studied in
Duan:2021ges
. However, those are not related to twisted affine Lie algebras, thus are not the concern of the current paper.

Some typical examples of twisted circle compactification include the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)
pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
SCFT which compactifies to the 5d
\mathfrak{su}(3)
gauge theory with Chern-Simons level 9, and the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist of 6d
(1,0)
pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)
SCFT which compactifies to 5d
\mathfrak{su}(4)
gauge theory with Chern-Simons level 8. The 5d KK theories are not necessarily gauge theories. For example, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)
pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)
SCFT is known to be a 5d non-Lagrangian theory. We will study the twisted elliptic genera for all these examples in this paper.

In the following, we summarize several approaches to the twisted elliptic genera.

•

Localization of 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs. Elliptic genera of some special 2d theories can be exactly computed by 2d localization, i.e., Jeffrey-Kirwan residue, analogous to the ADHM construction in 0d
Benini:2013nda
;
Benini:2013xpa
. This approach can be generalized to some special
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted theories
Kim:2021cua
. In general, this method when applicable is the most efficient way to compute (twisted) elliptic genera to arbitrary number of strings. Two known examples are the
\mathfrak{n}=3,

\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory and the
\mathfrak{n}=4,

\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})^{(2)}
theories which we will review in Section
2.4
.

•

\Gamma_{1}(N)
modular ansatz. The modular ansatz method exploits the modularity of elliptic genera
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
Gu:2017ccq
;
DelZotto:2017mee
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
;
Kim:2018gak
, inspired by the early work on compact elliptic Calabi-Yau threefolds
Huang:2015sta
. In particular, the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
modular ansatz for the one-string elliptic genera of rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs has been extensively studied in
DelZotto:2018tcj
. On the other hand, the modular ansatz of congruence subgroups
\Gamma_{0}(N)
and
\Gamma_{1}(N)
has been investigated in
Cota:2019cjx
;
Knapp:2021vkm
;
Schimannek:2021pau
for topological strings on genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds with
N
-section. Recently, the genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds associated with twisted affine Lie algebras were discussed in
paultalk
. We combine these results together to propose a suitable
\Gamma_{1}(N)
modular ansatz for the one-string twisted elliptic genera and successfully fix the ansatz for almost all rank-one twisted theories. Interestingly, we find that for almost all rank-one twisted theories, the modular group is enhanced from
\Gamma_{1}(N)
to
\Gamma_{0}(N)
.

•

Brane webs and topological vertex. Many 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs allow brane-web interpretations. When the brane-web construction exists, one can use the (refined) topological vertex to compute the 6d partition function. This method can be cleverly extended to some
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted theories by including O5-planes
Hayashi:2020hhb
;
Kim:2021cua
;
Kim:2022dbr
. For example, the 5d
\mathfrak{su}(3)_{9}
partition function can be computed by an extension of topological vertex called O-vertex

Hayashi:2020hhb
;
Nawata:2021dlk
.

•

Twisting from Higgsing. It has been noticed that some 6d twisted theories can be obtained by the Higgsing from 6d untwisted theories
Kim:2019dqn
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
. This generalizes the known Higgsing trees for 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, see e.g.
DelZotto:2018tcj
. Most known examples of twisting from Higgsing come from
\mathfrak{so}({2r})^{(2)}
type of theories with
\mathfrak{n}=2,3,4

Kim:2019dqn
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
. We will systematically study the twisting from Higgsing phenomenon for all rank-one theories, including the Higgsing from both untwisted theories and twisted ones. We will also propose a simple algebraic method to determine the precise Higgsing conditions based on representation decompositions.

•

Twisted elliptic blowup equations and the recursion formula. This will be the main computational method in the current paper. In a series of works
Gu:2018gmy
;
Gu:2019dan
;
Gu:2019pqj
;
Gu:2020fem
, the elliptic blowup equations have been established for the elliptic genera of all 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, which generalized Nakajima-Yoshioka’s blowup equations in 4d and 5d
Nakajima:2003pg
;
Nakajima:2005fg
;
Gottsche:2006bm
;
Nakajima:2009qjc
;
Keller:2012da
;
Kim:2019uqw
. Now we further generalize these very efficient and universal functional equations to all 6d rank-one twisted theories, both unity and vanishing blowup equations. We focus on the elliptic form, rather than the 5d form or geometric form of blowup equations, such that the twisted elliptic genera can be directly computed. Some unity blowup equations for some simple twisted theories have been studied in
Kim:2020hhh
.

This paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we give an overview on some salient features of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs and their twisted circle compactification. We will define twisted elliptic genera of the BPS strings and describe a fundamental quantity – the modular index. In Section
3
, we generalize the elliptic blowup equations in previous works to the twisted cases and derive a recursive formula to compute the twisted elliptic genera efficiently. In Section
4
, we focus on twisted one-string elliptic genera and study their universal features. In particular, we generalize the spectral flow symmetry of the one-string elliptic genera of 2d
(0,4)
theories discovered by Del Zotto and Lockhart
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
to the twisted cases. In Section
5
, we exploit the modularity of twisted elliptic genera, and propose the proper modular ansatz with modular group
\Gamma_{1}(N)
with
N=2,3,4
. In Section
6
, we discuss the Higgsing relations among untwisted/twisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs. In Section
7
, we summarize our results and point out some further directions.

Our conventions for simple Lie algebras and affine Lie algebras are the same with
Gu:2020fem
, see Appendix A therein. We often use
\mathfrak{su},\mathfrak{so}
and
\mathfrak{sp}
for classical gauge algebras where
\mathfrak{sp}(r)
is equivalent to
C_{r}
. We will also use
A,B,C,D
for more algebraic contexts. We frequently use
q=e^{2\pi i\tau}
,
v=e^{2\pi i\epsilon_{+}}
and
x=e^{2\pi i\epsilon_{-}}
as multiplicative variables following
DelZotto:2018tcj
, where
\epsilon_{\pm}=(\epsilon_{1}\pm\epsilon_{2})/2
.

2
General theory

2.1
Review of rank one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs

In this section, we briefly review some known results on the rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs established in
Morrison:2012np
;
Heckman:2013pva
;
Heckman:2015bfa
. More extensive reviews can be found in e.g.
Heckman:2018jxk
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
;
Gu:2020fem
.

The rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs on the tensor branch are the natural elliptic lift of 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
and 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theories with 8 supercharges. They are geometrically engineered by compactifying F-theory on certain elliptic non-compact Calabi-Yau threefolds
Morrison:1996na
;
Morrison:1996pp
. To be precise, these Calabi-Yau threefolds can be realized as elliptic fibration over some non-compact base surfaces
\mathcal{O}(-{\mathfrak{n}})\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The self-intersection number
-\mathfrak{n}
of the base curve
\mathbb{P}^{1}
can only take values from 1 to 8 and 12. This
\mathfrak{n}
is also called the tensor coefficient. The Kodaira and Tate singularity type of the elliptic fibration gives the 6d gauge algebra
{G}
which is supported on the
(-\mathfrak{n})
base curve. The 6d global flavor symmetry
{F}
on the other hand is supported on a non-compact curve intersecting with the
(-\mathfrak{n})
-curve. The 6d matters, i.e., hypermultiplets in representation
{{R}}
sit at the intersection point between curves
Katz:1996xe
. The
{(\mathfrak{n},G,{R})}
together are highly constrained by the Calabi-Yau condition. All possibilities have been classified in
Morrison:2012np
;
Heckman:2013pva
;
Heckman:2015bfa
. For example, there exist in total six pure gauge theories shortly denoted by
\mathfrak{n}_{G}
as
3_{\mathfrak{su}(3)},4_{\mathfrak{so}(8)},5_{F_{4}},6_{E_{6}},8_{E_{7}}
and
12_{E_{8}}
. Together with a
(7,{E_{7}},\frac{1}{2}\bf 56)
theory, these are the rank-one non-Higgsable clusters which serve as the “atoms” to be linked together to build higher rank 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs such as conformal matter theories
DelZotto:2014hpa
. Notably, the
4_{\mathfrak{so}(8)}
theory can be extended to an infinite series of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}=4
which are the
\mathfrak{so}(r+8)+r\bf V
theories. The full list of rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
{(\mathfrak{n},G,F,{R})}
can be found in e.g.
(
DelZotto:2018tcj
, Tables 20, 21)
. We collect those whose gauge algebra allows an outer automorphism i.e. type
A,D
and
E_{6}
in Table
1
, which will be our starting theories to perform twisted circle compactification.

Interestingly, it was noticed in
Gu:2020fem
that all rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
, gauge algebra
G
and
N_{i}
number of matters in representation
R_{i}^{G}
satisfy the following constraint

c=\frac{h_{G}^{\vee}}{6}-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{12}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{G})=\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2}{2}.

(2.4)

Here
\mathrm{Ind}
is the quadratic index of a representation.
The identity shows that the combination in the left hand side depends only on the self-intersection number
-\mathfrak{n}
of the base curve. This value will be called
the
c
constant
in the current paper. The physical meaning of
-c
is the left-moving Casimir energy in the Ramond sector of one BPS string. It also has a geometric meaning in elliptic non-compact Calabi-Yau threefolds and can be computed from the intersection theory
Cota:2019cjx
. Later we will also propose an interesting generalization of the
c
constant for the twisted theories.

Table 1:
Gauge, flavor symmetries and matter contents of all rank-one 6d SCFTs
DelZotto:2018tcj
with gauge algebra allowing an outer automorphism. The subscript in a flavor symmetry indicates the level
k_{F}
of the associated current algebra
F
. Matters are presented as the gauge and flavor representations of the half-hypermultiplets. The theories in gray have unpaired matter content upon
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist.

The 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs contain tensionless strings which we call BPS strings resembling the features of instantons in 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
and 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
gauge theories. Simple examples include E-strings which are realized by M2-branes stretched between a M5-brane and a M9-brane in the Horava-Witten picture of M-theory on
S^{1}/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
. The worldsheet theories on
k
E-strings can be realized as a series of 2d
(0,4)

O(k)
quiver gauge theories
Kim:2014dza
. The E-string theory is the simplest 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, which has no gauge symmetry but a
E_{8}
flavor symmetry. It can be geometrically engineered by compactifying F-theory on local half-K3 Calabi-Yau threefolds. The E-string theory can be extended to two infinite series of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}=1
which are
\mathfrak{sp}(N)+(2N+8)\bf F
and
\mathfrak{su}(N)+(N+8)\mathbf{F}+\bf\Lambda^{2}
theories.
Another simple example of BPS strings is the M-strings which are realized by M2-branes stretched between two M5-branes
Haghighat:2013gba
. In this case, the 6d supersymmetry is enhanced to
(2,0)
and the worldsheet theories become
(0,8)
. The worldsheet theories of
k
M-strings can also be realized as a series of 2d quiver gauge theories
Haghighat:2013gba
. The M-string theory can be extended to an infinite series of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}=2
which are
\mathfrak{su}(N)+2N\bf F
theories. In general, the BPS strings in a 6d
(1,0)
SCFT have worldsheet theories as rather nontrivial 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs. Only in some special cases, these exist known 2d quiver gauge theory constructions. These include the four infinite series of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,4
mentioned earlier.

The 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs usually contain three types of supermultiplets: the tensor, vector and hyper multiplets. As we are only interested in rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs in the current work, there is always only one tensor multiplet with tensor coefficients
\mathfrak{n}
. For all rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs except E-string theory, there is a nontrivial gauge algebra
G
and the vector multiplets are in the adjoint representation
\bf Adj
of
G
. When there is nontrivial matter content, the hypermultiplets are in the representation
R
of
G
. If we take into consideration the flavor symmetry
F
, the matter representations are often denoted as
(R^{G},R^{F})
of the half-hypermultiplets. The partition function of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs contains three parts: the classical part, the one-loop part and the elliptic genera of BPS strings. The first two parts can be directly written down once the data
(n,G,R)
are known, while the elliptic genera are much more nontrivial and difficult to compute.

One main task in the study of 6d SCFTs is to compute the elliptic genera of the 2d
(0,4)
worldsheet theories of the BPS strings. In the past decade, a huge amount of effort has been made and plenty of methods have been developed, see the summaries in e.g.
Gu:2019dan
;
Gu:2020fem
. Nevertheless, there still remain quite some rank-one 6d SCFTs whose elliptic genera we could not compute to the extent we would like. The existing major approaches include 2d localization, modular bootstrap, elliptic blowup equations, topological vertex and brane webs and so on. Each has their merits and limitations. For example, 2d localization is the most efficient method for arbitrary number of strings yet only applicable to those cases with known 2d quiver gauge theory descriptions. Elliptic blowup equations imply recursion formulas for elliptic genera or BPS invariants yet only applicable to the cases without half-hyper.
All these approaches have counterparts for twisted elliptic genera, which we will discuss in detail later.

The circle reduction of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs produces the 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theories with the same gauge group and matter content. For 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}\geq 3
, by sending
\tau\to i\infty
, i.e.,
q\to 0
, the
k
string elliptic genera of 6d SCFTs exactly reduce to the
k
instanton Nekrasov partition function of the 5d gauge theories.
For the
\mathfrak{n}=1,2
cases, there are some subtitles for the circle reduction such that the limit of elliptic genera and 5d Nekrasov partition function can have some small differences, which we refer to the discussion in
(
DelZotto:2018tcj
, Section 7)
. The circle reduction should not be confused with the circle compactification where all KK modes are kept. The circle compactification leads to 5d KK theories which are not necessarily gauge theories, while circle reduction leads to 5d gauge theories as deformation of 5d SCFTs.

2.2
Twisted circle compactification

It was conjectured in
Jefferson:2018irk
that all 5d SCFTs can be obtained by RG flows from 5d Kaluza-Klein (KK) theories, while the KK theories can be obtained from the compactifications of 6d SCFTs. When a 6d SCFT is compactified on a circle, one can turn on non-trivial discrete holonomies for the gauge fields from the discrete global symmetries, which are realized in
Bhardwaj:2019fzv
as combinations of the outer automorphisms of gauge algebras
G
and permutations of tensor multiplets. Such a circle compactification is usually called the twisted circle compactification
Bhardwaj:2019fzv
. In this paper, we are interested in the twisted circle compactification of rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, with a single tensor multiplet which is invariants under any kind of discrete global symmetries, so the outer automorphism of the gauge algebra
G
determines the possible types of twist. In fact, such automorphisms have been classified in the study of twisted affine Lie algebras
kac1990infinite
. Denote
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
as the order
r_{\mathrm{out}}
outer automorphism. Then
G=A_{2r-1},D_{r},E_{6}
admit order two outer automorphisms while
G=D_{4}
admits an order-three outer automorphism which act on the simple roots in patterns as described in Figure
1
.

A_{2}^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{2}
:
A_{2}^{(2)}
:

:

:

:

:

:
D_{l+1}^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{2}
:

:
D_{4}^{(1)},\mathcal{O}_{3}
:

:

:

Figure 1:
Dynkin diagrams

Upon twisted circle compactification, the gauge algebra of affine Lie type
G^{(1)}
in the tensor branch effectively becomes the twisted affine Lie algebra
G^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
. The vector multiplets are in the representation described by the roots of the twisted affine algebra, which are described by the representations of the finite dimensional subalgebra
\mathring{G}
obtained from outer automorphism folding of
G
with KK charge
\tau
. The Dynkin diagram of
\mathring{G}
is obtained by simply removing the affine node of the Dynkin diagram of
G^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
. Denote
n_{G}
as the rank of the automorphism of
G
, for
G=A_{2r-1},D_{r},E_{6}
, the inner automorphism is trivial, such that
n_{G}
is equal to the rank of outer automorphism. The roots of
G^{(n_{G})}
are

\displaystyle\Delta=\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}\cup\Delta^{\mathrm{re}},

(2.5)

where

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{k}{n_{G}}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},k\neq 0\right\},

(2.6)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\alpha+\frac{k}{n_{G}}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}\right\}\cup\left\{\alpha+{k}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}\right\},

(2.7)

where
\mathring{\Delta}_{s}
and
\mathring{\Delta}_{l}
are the sets of short roots and long roots of
\mathring{G}
. When
G=A_{2l}
, there is a nontrivial inner automorphism such that the whole rank of the automorphism is
n_{G}=4
, the roots of
A_{2r}^{(2)}
are

\displaystyle\Delta=\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}\cup\Delta^{\mathrm{re}},

(2.8)

where

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathrm{im}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{k}{2}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},k\neq 0\right\},

(2.9)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mathrm{re}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\frac{1}{2}\alpha+\frac{1}{4}(2k-1)\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}\right\}\cup\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}{k}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\ \,\cup\left\{\alpha+{k}\tau|k\in\mathbb{Z},\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}\right\},

(2.10)

where
\mathring{\Delta}_{s}
and
\mathring{\Delta}_{l}
are the sets of short roots and long roots of
C_{r}
.
We summarize all KK-momentum shifts of adjoint representations under twist in Table
2
.

Table 3:
Gauge algebra, matter representation and effective flavor symmetry for all twisted 6d
(1,0)
rank-one theories with paired matter contents. Here
\mathring{R}=(\mathring{R}_{\mathring{G}},\mathring{R}_{\mathring{F}})
and we only write down
\mathring{R}_{\mathring{G}}
for short. The important value
c
will be discussed later.

For 6d SCFTs with hypermultiplets in the representations of the flavor algebra
F
, the representations have integer KK charges for a circle compactification without twist. For twisted circle compactifications, the hypermultiplets are in the representations of the sub flavor algebra
\mathring{F}
, with fractional KK charges. We refer to the Reference A of
Kim:2020hhh
for a detailed discussion. The twisted matter representations for all twisted 6d
(1,0)
rank-one theories with paired matter contents are summarized in Table
3
. For a twisted theory with gauge group
G
and non-trivial matter representations, they always take the form

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i}N_{i}(\mathring{R}_{i,0}\oplus\mathring{R}_{i,1/n^{G}_{i}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathring{R}_{i,(n^{G}_{i}-1)/n^{G}_{i}}),

(2.11)

where
n^{G}_{i}
is either the rank of outer automorphism
r_{\mathrm{out}}
or
1
. We denote a representation with zero KK charge
R_{i,0}
as
R_{i}
, and
N_{i}
is the number of matters in the representation
R_{i}
.

2.3
Twisted prepotential and one-loop free energy

The prepotential, which is also the classical part of the genus zero free energy of the corresponding topological strings, contains the tree-level and one-loop level contributions

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(0,0)}=\mathcal{F}^{\text{tree}}+\mathcal{F}^{\text{1-loop}}.

(2.12)

The tree level contribution contains the information of tensor multiplet, which should not be changed for the twisted circle compactification of rank-one theories. Under
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
action, we expect that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\text{tree}}

\displaystyle=\frac{t_{\text{ell}}}{2\mathfrak{n}}\bigg(-\mathfrak{n}\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}(m_{G}\cdot m_{G})+\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}\sum_{i}(k_{F_{i}}m_{F_{i}}\cdot m_{F_{i}})\bigg)-\frac{1}{2\mathfrak{n}}t_{\text{ell}}^{2}\tau

\displaystyle=\frac{t_{\text{ell}}}{2\mathfrak{n}}\bigg(-\mathfrak{n}m_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+\sum_{i}k_{\mathring{F}_{i}}m_{\mathring{F}_{i}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}_{i}}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2\mathfrak{n}}t_{\text{ell}}^{2}\tau,

(2.13)

where the
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
acts on the bilinear form
m\cdot m
by identifying the masses in the orbit of
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
, such that it becomes the bilinear form of the algebra
\mathring{G}/\mathring{F}
. The
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
acts on
k_{F_{i}}
by multiplying it to
k_{\mathring{F}_{i}}=n^{G}_{i}k_{F_{i}}
, which can be understood as follows. For the case without twist,
k_{F}
is the intersection number of the non-compact curve and the base curve
Gu:2020fem
. When we do the outer automorphism twist, the non-compact curves that along the obit of the action
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
should be identified, which naturally changes the intersection number from
k_{F_{i}}
to
k_{\mathring{F}_{i}}=n^{G}_{i}k_{F_{i}}
.

As suggested in
Kim:2020hhh
, the one-loop prepotential can be evaluated from the zeta regularization of the summation over infinite roots or weights of the twisted affine algebra. For
{G}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
not of type
A_{2r}^{(2)}
, we have
n_{G}=r_{\mathrm{out}}
, and the one loop prepotential can be formally written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{1\text{-loop}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}}|\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau|^{3}-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}}|\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau/{n_{G}}|^{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,f_{i}}\sum_{w_{\mathring{G}}\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{G}}}|w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau/n^{G}_{i}+m_{f_{i}}|^{3},

(2.14)

where
\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{G}}
are the representations of the matter contents with KK charge zero. Using the zeta regularization
1
1

1

The formal definition of zeta function
\zeta(s)=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n^{s}}
for
\mathrm{Re}(s)>1
can be extended to the whole complex plane via analytic continuation.

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}1=\zeta(0)=-\frac{1}{2},\quad\quad\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k=\zeta(-1)=-\frac{1}{12},\quad\quad\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}k^{2}=\zeta(-2)=0,

(2.15)

the relevant parts in the one-loop prepotential become

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}}|\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau|^{3}\sim-\frac{1}{6}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}^{+}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{3}+\frac{1}{24}\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{l}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2},

(2.16)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}}|\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau/{n_{G}}|^{3}\sim-\frac{1}{6}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}^{+}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{3}+\frac{1}{24n_{G}}\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}_{s}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2},

(2.17)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i,f_{i}}\sum_{w_{\mathring{G}}\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{G}}}|w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k\tau/n^{G}_{i}+m_{f_{i}}|^{3}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\sim\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i}\sum_{w\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}})^{3}-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{24n^{G}_{i}}\tau\cdot\sum_{w_{\mathring{G}}\in\mathring{R}_{i,r_{i}}^{\mathring{G}}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2}.

(2.18)

Then the total one-loop prepotential becomes

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{1\text{-loop}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}^{+}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{3}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i}\sum_{w\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}})^{3}

(2.19)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{24}\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2}+\frac{1-n_{G}}{24n_{G}}\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{s}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2}-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{24n^{G}_{i}}\tau\cdot\sum_{w_{\mathring{G}}\in\mathring{R}_{i,r_{i}}^{G}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{6}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}^{+}}(\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}})^{3}+\frac{1}{12}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{i}\sum_{w\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}})^{3}+c\tau m_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}.

Here we define the positive weights as

\displaystyle\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}=\{w_{\mathring{G}}\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{G}},w_{\mathring{F}}\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{F}}\,\big|\,w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}}>0\},

(2.20)

and the constant
c
as

\displaystyle c=\frac{h_{\mathring{G}}^{\vee}}{6}-\frac{n_{G}-1}{12n_{G}}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{\mathring{G}})-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{12n^{G}_{i}}\mathrm{Ind}(\mathring{R}_{i}^{\mathring{G}}).

(2.21)

For the
A_{2r}^{(2)}
cases, as described in (
2.9
), there are additional “shortest” roots taking the KK-charge
n+\frac{1}{4}
and
n+\frac{3}{4}
. They contribute an additional term

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{12}\times 2\times 3\times\left(\zeta(-1,\frac{1}{4})+\zeta(-1,\frac{3}{4})\right)\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}^{+}}\left(\frac{\alpha\cdot t}{2}\right)^{2}=-\frac{1}{96}\tau\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}^{+}}\left(\frac{\alpha\cdot t}{2}\right)^{2},

(2.22)

which contributes to
c
by
-\frac{1}{96}\times 2\,\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{F})=-\frac{1}{48}
. Therefore, for
A_{2r}^{(2)}
theory with
N
KK zero mode matter in the fundamental representation, the one-loop prepotential takes the form of the last line in (
2.19
) with

\displaystyle c=\frac{r+1}{6}-\frac{1}{12}(r-1)-\frac{1}{48}-\frac{N}{24}\times 1=\frac{1}{48}(11+4r)-\frac{1}{24}N.

(2.23)

Here we have used that for
G=C_{r}
,
h_{G}^{\vee}=r+1
,
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{F})=1
and
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2})=2(r-1)
.

By explicit computation for all twisted theories, we observe the following interesting identities of the
c
constant. For all the cases with twist coefficients
n_{G}=2,3,4
in Table
3
,

\boxed{c=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{2n_{G}}-\frac{1}{(n_{G})^{2}},\qquad\mathring{G}={B_{r},C_{r}},\\
\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{2(n_{G})^{2}}+\frac{1}{n_{G}},\qquad\mathring{G}={G_{2},F_{4}}.\end{array}\right.}

(2.24)

The physical meaning of the constant
c
is (the negative of) the left-moving Casimir energy in the twisted Ramond sector which will be discussed later. In geometry,
c
can be absorbed by the base Kähler parameter. A similar shift of the base Kähler parameters has been discussed in
Cota:2019cjx
, which is closely related to the intersection number of the divisors in the genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds. It is interesting to construct the geometry explicitly and reproduce the identity (
2.24
).

In order to make connection to the blowup equation in Section
3
, we need the information about the classical part of the genus one free energies
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(0,1)}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(1,0)}
. As described in Section 2 of
Gu:2020fem
, these two functions only contain bilinear forms of the gauge and flavor algebra and the part related to the tensor multiplet. The action of the automorphism twist maps the bilinear forms to the bilinear forms of the folded algebras directly and leaves the tensor part unchanged. We expect that

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(0,1)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}^{+}}\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i}\sum_{w\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}})+\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2}{2\mathfrak{n}}t_{\mathrm{ell}},

(2.25)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(1,0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{12}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathring{\Delta}^{+}}\alpha\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i}\sum_{w\in\mathring{R}_{i}^{+}}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}})+\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2-h^{\vee}_{G^{(r)}}}{4\mathfrak{n}}t_{\mathrm{ell}}.

(2.26)

Notice that the dual Coxeter number
h_{G}^{\vee}
in (
2.26
) is the number for the original group
G
or the twisted group
G^{(r)}
, according to the identity

\displaystyle h^{\vee}_{G^{(r)}}=h^{\vee}_{G^{(1)}}=h_{G}^{\vee}.

(2.27)

This is easy to understand because the dual Coxeter number is the summation of all comarks of the affine Dynkin diagrams and the comark of each node of twisted affine Lie algebra just comes from the summation of the comarks of the nodes folded together.

2.4
Twisted elliptic genera of BPS strings

The Ramond-Ramond (R-R) elliptic genus of the BPS strings in 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs is defined as the trace over the Hilbert space of the R-R sector of the 2d
(0,4)
worldsheet theory:

\mathbb{E}(q,v,x,m)=\mathrm{Tr}_{\rm RR}(-1)^{F_{L}+F_{R}}e^{2\pi i\tau H_{L}}e^{2\pi i\bar{\tau}H_{R}}x^{J_{L}^{3}}v^{J_{R}^{3}+J_{I}^{3}}\prod e^{2\pi im\cdot K},

(2.28)

where
m
and
K
are the fugacities and the Cartan generators associated to the 2d global symmetry, which is composed of 6d gauge and flavor symmetries. The
J_{L}^{3},J_{R}^{3}
and
J_{I}^{3}
are the usual Cartan generators of the little group
\mathfrak{so}(4)=\mathfrak{su}(2)_{L}\times\mathfrak{su}(2)_{R}
and the R-symmetry group
\mathfrak{su}(2)_{I}
of the 6d
(1,0)
SCFT. It is common to refer to the R-R elliptic genera as just
elliptic genera
. One can also consider the trace over the Hilbert space of the Neveu-Schwarz-Ramond (NS-R) sector of the 2d theory, in which case the above definition gives the NS-R elliptic genera. This type of elliptic genera will also be used in later sections when we discuss the spectral flow symmetry.

In the case of twisted circle compactification of 6d SCFTs, the 2d worldsheet theories also get twisted circle compactified on one circle of the torus. We can formally define the twisted elliptic genera by replacing the Hilbert space in (
2.28
) to the Hilbert space of the 2d states appearing in the twisted circle compactification. The Hamiltonians
H_{L}
now have fractional eigenvalues due to the twist. For the twist coefficient
n_{G}=2,3,4
, the gap between energy eigenvalues becomes
1/n_{G}
. This brings in many new fascinating features for the twisted elliptic genera. We find the primary feature of
d
-string twisted elliptic genera is

\mathbb{E}_{d}(q,v,x,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})=q^{-dc}\Big(Z_{d}^{5d}(v,x,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})+\mathcal{O}(q^{1/n_{G}})\Big).

(2.29)

Here
Z_{d}^{5d}
is the
d
-instanton Nekrasov partition function of the circle reduction, i.e., the 5d low energy theory of the twisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFT. Such a 5d theory has gauge group
\mathring{G}
and the 5d matter content taken from the KK-charge 0 part of the 6d twisted matter content.

When the 2d
(0,4)
worldsheet theory has a known 2d quiver gauge theory construction, the above definition can be explicitly computed by 2d localization, also known as Jeffrey-Kirwan residue
Benini:2013nda
;
Benini:2013xpa
. However, this good situation happens very rarely. In most cases, it is very hard or impossible to construct a 2d gauge theory for the worldsheet theory of BPS strings. For the twisted cases, this is even hard and rare.
In this section, we review the 2d localization formulas for 6d
\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})^{(2)}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=4
and pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=3
, which were first established in
Kim:2021cua
. As far as we know, these are the only two types of twisted theories, for which the twisted elliptic genera can be computed directly.

\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})
theory with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist

The instanton partition function of the
\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})^{(2)}
theory can be obtained from the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})
theory with
2r
fundamental flavors. In the 2d
\mathcal{N}=(0,4)
quiver gauge theory description of the 6d
\mathfrak{so}({2r+8})
theory, at
k
-strings, the resulting theory is a
\mathfrak{sp}(k)
gauge theory coupled to the bulk
\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)
gauge group and the
\mathfrak{sp}(2r)
flavor group as follows

\mathfrak{sp}(k)
\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)
\mathfrak{sp}(2r)
asym
1

(2.30)

Here solid/dashed lines denote 2d hypermultiplets/Fermi multiplets respectively. Under the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
outer automorphism twist, the
\mathfrak{sp}(k)
gauge algebra and
\mathfrak{sp}(2r)
flavor algebra keep invariant, so we only need to take care of the bulk gauge group
\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)
. In the 2d description, the bulk gauge group serves as a flavor group and it couples with the gauge group
\mathfrak{sp}(k)
in the fundamental representation. Under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist, the fundamental representation of
\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)
is split into the fundamental representation of
\mathfrak{so}(2r+7)
with KK charge zero and a trivial representation of
\mathfrak{so}(2r+7)
with KK charge one half. The 6d partition function turns out to be
Kim:2021cua
;
Chen:2021rek

\displaystyle Z_{\text{str}}^{\rm{6d}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{q}_{\phi}^{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{k},

(2.31)

where
\mathbb{E}_{k}
is the
k
-string elliptic genus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}=\frac{1}{2^{k}k!}\oint\prod_{I=1}^{k}\frac{{\rm d}u_{I}}{2\pi i}\cdot Z_{k,1-\text{loop}}^{\rm{6d}}(u_{I}),

(2.32)

with the integrand to be

\displaystyle Z_{k,1-\text{loop}}^{\rm{6d}}(u_{I})=

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{\vartheta_{1}\left(2\epsilon_{+}\right)}{\vartheta_{1}\left(\epsilon_{1,2}\right)}\right)^{k}\cdot\prod_{I=1}^{k}\vartheta_{1}\left(\pm 2u_{I}\right)\vartheta_{1}\left(2\epsilon_{+}\pm 2u_{I}\right)

(2.33)

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{I<J}^{k}\frac{\vartheta_{1}\left(\pm u_{I}\pm u_{J}\right)\vartheta_{1}\left(2\epsilon_{+}\pm u_{I}\pm u_{J}\right)}{\vartheta_{1}\left(\epsilon_{1}\pm u_{I}\pm u_{J}\right)\vartheta_{1}\left(\epsilon_{2}\pm u_{I}\pm u_{J}\right)}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{I=1}^{k}\frac{\prod_{l=1}^{2r}\vartheta_{1}\left(\pm u_{I}+m_{l}\right)}{\vartheta_{1}\left(\epsilon_{+}\pm u_{I}\right)\vartheta_{4}\left(\epsilon_{+}\pm u_{I}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{r+3}\vartheta_{1}\left(\epsilon_{+}\pm u_{I}\pm\alpha_{i}\right)}\right)\,,

where we define the variant theta functions
\vartheta_{i}(z)={\theta_{i}(z,\tau)}/{\eta(\tau)},i=1,\cdots,4
. Here the
\vartheta_{4}
in the last line comes from the trivial representation of
\mathfrak{so}(2r+7)
with
\frac{1}{2}
KK charge.

\mathfrak{su}(3)
theory with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist

It was realized in
Kim:2021cua
from brane webs that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted circle compactification of 6d pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
can be obtained from Higgsing of 6d
G_{2}
gauge theory with one fundamental flavor. The 2d description of 6d
G_{2}
theory with one fundamental has been constructed in
Kim:2018gjo
, where the theory is written from the
G_{2}
subalgebra
\mathfrak{su}(3)
. Note the
G_{2}
algebra contains a subalgebra
\mathfrak{su}(2)\times\mathfrak{su}(2)
. Denote
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
the Coulomb parameters for the
\mathfrak{su}(2)
’s, and
m
the mass for the fundamental flavor in the 6d
G_{2}
theory, we have the following tuning of parameters for the Higgsing

\displaystyle\alpha\rightarrow\alpha,\quad\alpha^{\prime}\rightarrow\frac{\tau}{4},\quad m\rightarrow\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{2}.

(2.34)

Apply the reparametrization (
2.34
), we have the
k
-string elliptic genus of 6d
\mathfrak{su}(3)
theory with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist as

\displaystyle Z_{\text{str}}^{\rm{6d}}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}{q}_{\phi}^{k}\,\mathbb{E}_{k},

(2.35)

where
\mathbb{E}_{k}
is the
k
-string elliptic genus

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}=\frac{1}{k!}\oint\prod_{I=1}^{k}\frac{{\rm d}u_{I}}{2\pi i}\cdot Z_{k,1-\text{loop}}^{\rm{6d}}(u_{I}),

(2.36)

with the integrand in the elliptic form to be

\displaystyle Z_{k,1-\text{loop}}^{\rm{6d}}(u_{I})=\prod_{I=1}^{k}\frac{d{u}_{I}}{2\pi i}\cdot\frac{\prod_{I\neq J}\vartheta_{1}({u_{IJ}})\cdot\prod_{I,J}\vartheta_{1}({2\epsilon_{+}-{u}_{IJ}})}{\prod_{I=1}^{k}\prod_{i=1}^{2}{\vartheta_{1}}({\epsilon_{+}\pm({u}_{I}-\alpha_{i})})\cdot\prod_{I,J}\vartheta_{1}({\epsilon_{1,2}+{u}_{IJ}})}

(2.37)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\prod_{I}\!\big(\vartheta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}({\epsilon_{+}+{u}_{I}})\cdot\prod_{I\leq J}\big(\vartheta_{4}({{u}_{I}+{u}_{J}})\cdot\vartheta_{4}({{u}_{I}+{u}_{J}-2\epsilon_{+}})\big)}{\prod_{I}\!\big(\vartheta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}(\epsilon_{+}-{u}_{I})\vartheta_{4}^{[\mp\frac{1}{4}]}({\epsilon_{+}\pm{u}_{I}})\prod_{i=1}^{2}\vartheta_{4}({\epsilon_{+}-{u}_{I}-\alpha_{i}})\big)\cdot\prod_{I<J}\vartheta_{4}({\epsilon_{1,2}-{u}_{I}-{u}_{J}})},

where
\alpha_{1}=-\alpha_{2}=\alpha
.
The residue sum can be labeled by
\mathfrak{su}(2)
colored Young diagrams, then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{k}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\vec{Y};|\vec{Y}|=k}\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s\in Y_{i}}\frac{\vartheta_{4}(2{u}(s))\cdot\vartheta_{4}(2\epsilon_{+}-2{u}(s))}{\prod_{j=1}^{2}\left(\vartheta_{1}({E_{ij}(s)})\cdot\vartheta_{1}({E_{ij}(s)-2\epsilon_{+}})\right)}

(2.38)

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{2}\prod_{s\in Y_{i}}\frac{\vartheta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}(\epsilon_{+}+u(s))}{\vartheta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}(\epsilon_{+}-u(s))\vartheta_{4}^{[\mp\frac{1}{4}]}(\epsilon_{+}\pm u(s))\cdot\prod_{j=1}^{2}\vartheta_{4}({\epsilon_{+}-{u}(s)-\alpha_{j}})}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i\leq j}^{2}\prod_{s_{i,j}\in Y_{i,j};s_{i}<s_{j}}\frac{\vartheta_{4}({{u}(s_{i})+{u}(s_{j})})\cdot\vartheta_{4}({{u}(s_{i})+{u}(s_{j})-2\epsilon_{+}})}{\vartheta_{4}({\epsilon_{1,2}-{u}(s_{i})-{u}(s_{j})})},

where

{u}(s)=\alpha_{i}-\epsilon_{+}-(n-1)\epsilon_{1}-(m-1)\epsilon_{2},\quad s=(m,n)\in Y_{i},

(2.39)

and

E_{ij}(s)=\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{j}-\epsilon_{1}h_{i}(s)+\epsilon_{2}(v_{j}(s)+1),

(2.40)

Here
s_{i}\!<\!s_{j}
means (
i<j
) or (
i\!=\!j
and
m_{i}\!<\!m_{j}
) or
(
i\!=\!j
and
m_{i}\!=\!m_{j}
and
n_{i}\!<\!n_{j}
).
The
h_{i}(s)
denotes the distance from
s
to
the right end of the diagram
Y_{i}
by moving right and the
v_{j}(s)
denotes the distance from
s
to the bottom of the diagram
Y_{j}
by moving down.

2.5
Anomaly and modular index

Elliptic genera
\mathbb{E}
of 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs in general are weight zero Jacobi forms of multi elliptic variables on
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
. They are equipped with an important quantity – the modular index
\mathrm{Ind}\,\mathbb{E}
, as a quadratic form of the elliptic variables. Under modular transformation of the torus, the elliptic genera transform as

\mathbb{E}_{d}\Big(-\frac{1}{\tau},\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\tau},\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{\tau},\frac{m_{G}}{\tau},\frac{m_{F}}{\tau}\Big)=\exp{\Big(\frac{2\pi i}{\tau}\mathrm{Ind}\,\mathbb{E}\Big)}\mathbb{E}(\tau,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{G},m_{F}).

(2.41)

Equivalently, one can write as

\partial_{E_{2}}\log\mathbb{E}=-\frac{1}{12}(\mathrm{Ind}\,\mathbb{E}).

(2.42)

Here
E_{2}(\tau)
is the weight-two Eisenstein series that measures the modular anomaly.

For 6d rank-one
(1,0)
theories, the modular index of the elliptic genera can be derived from the anomaly polynomials for the 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs on the worldsheet of the BPS strings
Ohmori:2014pca
;
Ohmori:2014kda
;
Shimizu:2016lbw
. To be precise, for a 6d
(1,0)
theory with tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
, gauge group
G
and flavor group
F
, the modular index of the
d
-string elliptic genus can be uniformly written as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}\,\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{G},m_{F})=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}{2}\Big)^{2}(2-\mathfrak{n}+h_{G}^{\vee})d+\frac{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}{2}(\mathfrak{n}d^{2}+(2-\mathfrak{n})d)

(2.43)

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{2}(-\mathfrak{n}\,m_{G}\cdot m_{G}+k_{F}\,m_{F}\cdot m_{F})\ .

The modularity for twisted elliptic genera is more complicated. Though from the viewpoint of twisted circle compactification, it is convenient to discuss the fractional KK-charges, which brings in the fractional
q^{1/n_{G}}
orders in twisted elliptic genera, where
n_{G}=2,3,4
. From the geometric viewpoint, it is more suitable to scale
q
to
q^{n_{G}}
such that we are still in the integral bases of curves. After such scaling, it is natural to expect that the twisted elliptic genera behave as weight zero Jacobi forms of some congruence subgroups of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
. We propose that in general the congruence subgroup should be
\Gamma_{1}(n_{G})
, though in many cases, we notice that the twisted elliptic genera satisfy a bigger modular symmetry that is
\Gamma_{0}(n_{G})
. These are consistent with the geometric observation in
Cota:2019cjx
for multi-section Calabi-Yau threefolds. We collect the definition of various congruence subgroups of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
in Appendix
B
.

The index quadratic form does not really depend on the modular groups. For
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
twisted compactification of rank-one SCFTs, the tensor multiplet is invariant under the twist, the gauge and flavor algebras become their twisted versions due to the identification of the masses in the same orbits of
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
. This indicates that the modular index of twisted elliptic genus can be directly deduced from its untwisted origin by identifying the masses of the particles of the hyper and vector multiplets in the same orbits of
\mathcal{O}^{(r_{\mathrm{out}})}
. Thus we have the following uniform formula for the index of
d
-string twisted elliptic genus:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}\,\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}}{2}\Big)^{2}(2-\mathfrak{n}+h_{G}^{\vee})d+\frac{\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}}{2}(\mathfrak{n}d^{2}+(2-\mathfrak{n})d)

(2.44)

\displaystyle+\frac{d}{2}(-\mathfrak{n}\,m_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k_{\mathring{F}}\,m_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}}).

One can see that only the second line changes from (
2.43
). It should be emphasized that the dual Coxeter number
h_{G}^{\vee}
here does not change upon the twist, as was explained in the identity (
2.27
).

3
Twisted elliptic blowup equations

3.1
Elliptic blowup equations and their twists

For refined topological strings on a non-compact Calabi-Yau threefold
X
, the general form of blowup equations was proposed in
Huang:2017mis
:

\displaystyle\Lambda(\epsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{2},m_{i})Z\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},t_{i}+\pi{\mathsf{i}}B_{i}\right)

(3.45)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{k}\in\mathbb{Z}^{b_{4}}}

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\mathbf{k}|}Z\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1},t_{i}+(C_{ij}k_{j}+B_{i}/2)\epsilon_{1}+\pi{\mathsf{i}}B_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\times Z\left(\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2},t_{i}+(C_{ij}k_{j}+B_{i}/2)\epsilon_{2}+\pi{\mathsf{i}}B_{i}\right),

as a generalization of the K-theoretic blowup equations for 5d gauge theories in
Nakajima:2005fg
. Here
b_{4}
is the Betti number which counts the number of compact divisors of
X
. The
t_{i}
is the Kähler parameter which is the volume of the curve class in
H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})
and
C_{ij}
is the intersection numbers of divisors and curves in
X
. The
B_{i}
is the flux for the corresponding Kähler parameter, where the value can be determined from the classical prepotential of the topological strings on
X

Huang:2017mis
. The
\Lambda(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{i})
is a function that can be fixed from the fluxes
B_{i}
and the classical prepotential, and it only depends on the mass parameters of the theory.

In a series of works
Gu:2018gmy
;
Gu:2019dan
;
Gu:2019pqj
;
Gu:2020fem
;
Duan:2021ges
, elliptic blowup equations have been established for all 6d
(1,0)
and
(2,0)
SCFTs. These functional equations serve as very efficient tools to solve the elliptic genera of the BPS strings associated to 6d SCFTs. For example, for rank-one 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
\mathfrak{n}\geq 3
and no half-hyper, there exist a universal recursive formula for the elliptic genera with respect to the number of strings derived from unity elliptic blowup equations
Gu:2020fem
. In this section, we generalize both the elliptic blowup equations and the recursive formulas to the twisted cases. The partition functions of twisted circle compactification of 6d SCFTs can be written in terms of twisted elliptic genera as

\displaystyle Z\left(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},t_{i}\right)=e^{\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}}Z_{0}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})\bigg(1+\sum_{d=1}^{\infty}e^{d\cdot t_{\mathrm{ell}}}\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})\bigg),

(3.46)

where
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}
is the classical prepotential
\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}=\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(0,0)}+\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(0,1)}+\mathcal{F}^{\mathrm{cls}}_{(1,0)}
defined by (
2.12
), (
2.25
) and (
2.26
).
The derivation of twisted elliptic blowup equations from (
3.45
) to the elliptic form is exactly parallel to the untwisted cases, by a “de-affinization procedure”, thus we only present the results here. We refer readers interested in the derivation to Section 2.4 of
Gu:2019dan
and Section 3.1 and Appendix D of
Gu:2020fem
.

Consider a rank one 6d SCFT with tensor branch coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
,
gauge symmetry
G
, flavor symmetry
F
, and half-hypermultiplets
transforming in the representations
(R_{G},R_{F})
. The flavor
symmetry induces a current algebra of level
k_{F}
on the worldsheet of
BPS strings. Upon discrete twist, the twisted affine Lie algebra
\widehat{G}^{(n)}
has truncated part
\mathring{G}
as low energy 5d gauge symmetry. In the meantime, matter representation becomes
\mathring{R}
and reduced flavor symmetry becomes
\mathring{F}
. Let
\lambda_{0}
be a coweight base of
G
that is
invariant
upon the twist.
2
2

2

For example for
\mathfrak{so}(2r)^{(2)}
, the
\lambda_{0}
associated to the vector representation is invariant upon the twist, while the one associated to the spinor representation is not.
If
(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{F})
gives admissible elliptic blowup equation for the original 6d SCFT, and let
\lambda_{\mathring{F}}
be the reduction of
\lambda_{F}
, then we propose the twisted elliptic genera
\mathbb{E}_{d}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\epsilon_{1,2})

satisfy the following
twisted elliptic blowup equations
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\lambda_{\mathring{G}}\in\phi_{\lambda_{0}}(Q^{\vee}(\mathring{G}))}^{\frac{1}{2}||\lambda_{\mathring{G}}||^{2}+d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}=d+\delta}(-1)^{|\lambda_{\mathring{G}}|}

(3.47)

\displaystyle\times\theta_{i}^{[a]}(\mathfrak{n}\tau,-\mathfrak{n}\lambda_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+k_{\mathring{F}}\lambda_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}}+(y-\small{\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{2}}||\lambda_{\mathring{G}}||^{2})(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})-\mathfrak{n}d^{\prime}\epsilon_{1}-\mathfrak{n}d^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\times A_{{V}}^{\mathring{G}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})A_{{V}}^{frac}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})A_{{H}}^{\mathring{R}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{F}})

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}_{d^{\prime}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}}+\epsilon_{1}\lambda_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}}+\epsilon_{1}\lambda_{\mathring{F}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})\mathbb{E}_{d^{\prime\prime}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}}+\epsilon_{2}\lambda_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}}+\epsilon_{2}\lambda_{\mathring{F}},\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=\Lambda(\delta)\,\theta_{i}^{[a]}(\mathfrak{n}\tau,k_{F}\lambda_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}}+\mathfrak{n}y(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}))\mathbb{E}_{d}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}),\qquad d=0,1,2,\ldots

where

\Lambda(\delta)=\begin{cases}1,&\delta=0,\\
0,&\delta>0.\end{cases}

(3.48)

The
\Lambda(\delta)=1
case is called
unity
blowup equations, while the
\Lambda(\delta)=0
case
vanishing
. The parameter
y
is determined in
Gu:2020fem
as

y=\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2+h_{G}^{\vee}}{4}+\frac{k_{F}}{2}(\lambda_{F}\cdot\lambda_{F})=\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2+h_{G}^{\vee}}{4}+\frac{k_{\mathring{F}}}{2}(\lambda_{\mathring{F}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{F}}),

(3.49)

which does not change upon twist.
The Jacobi theta function
\theta_{i}^{[a]}
with characteristics
a
are defined in Appendix
A
.
It comes from the contribution of the prepotential of 6d twisted theories. The subscript
i
is 3 if
\mathfrak{n}
is even and 4 if
\mathfrak{n}
is odd. For twist coefficient
n_{G}=2,3
, the characteristic
a
of the theta function can be one of the following

\mathfrak{n}
numbers

2\mathfrak{n}a=h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i}{N_{i}}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{\mathring{G}})-2k,\quad k=0,1,\ldots,\mathfrak{n}-1.

(3.50)

Recall
N_{i}
are the number of hypers in
R_{i}^{\mathring{G}}
with KK-charge zero. This formula means that only the KK-charge 0 part is relevant for the characteristic
a
, which is required for the 5d limit of twisted elliptic blowup equations to be consistent with the 5d blowup equations with matters
Kim:2019uqw
. For twist coefficient
n_{G}=4
, the low energy gauge group
\mathring{G}=C_{r}
which needs special care. When there is no KK-charge 0 matter, the
C_{r}
allows another possibility called the
\theta=\pi
theory, in which case the
h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}
in (
3.50
) needs to be replaced as
h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}-1
. This is also consistent with the 5d blowup equations in
Kim:2019uqw
. The one-loop vector-multiplet contribution
A_{{V}}^{\mathring{G}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})
is defined as Equation
(3.7)
of
Gu:2020fem
, while the fractional part
A_{{V}}^{frac}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})
is defined similarly with the fractional KK-charge taken into account. The one-loop hypermultiplet contribution
A_{{H}}^{\mathring{R}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{F}})
is defined similar with Equation
(3.8)
of
Gu:2020fem
with KK-charges taken into account. We collect the relevant formulas in Appendix
A
. We summarize all admissible unity twisted elliptic blowup equations in Table
4
. We also collect some simple vanishing ones in Table
5
. It can be expected that there will be more vanishing blowup equations for some theories. Besides, we will focus on the twisted theories with paired matter content here. The blowup equations and twisted elliptic genera for theories with unpaired matter content are more subtle, we leave the discussion in Appendix
D
.

\mathfrak{so}(4r)

(0\ldots 0)

B_{3}

\mathfrak{sp}(2)\times\mathfrak{sp}(2)

(01),(00)

B_{4}

\mathfrak{sp}(4)\times\mathfrak{sp}(1)

B_{5}

\mathfrak{sp}(6)

D_{4}^{(2)}

B_{3}

\mathfrak{sp}(3)\times\mathfrak{sp}(3)
