(001),(000)

Table 5:
The parameters
y,\lambda_{\mathring{F}}
of vanishing twisted elliptic blowup equations for rank-one models. The # is the number of vanishing equations with fixed characteristics
a
.

To solve blowup equations, four methods have been presented in Section 4 of
Gu:2020fem
, which are recursion formula and Weyl orbit expansion for elliptic blowup equations, and refined BPS expansion and
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}
expansion for general local Calabi-Yau threefolds. All four methods apply to the current twisted cases as well.
In Section
3.3
, we will explicitly present the recursion formula for twisted elliptic genera, which is the most efficient approach but only works for
\mathfrak{n}\geq 3
theories. We also use the Weyl orbit expansion to solve the twisted elliptic genera of many
\mathfrak{n}=1,2
theories. Both methods utilize the unity part of (
3.47
).
The vanishing part of (
3.47
) is less useful in solving twisted elliptic genera, but still gives some interesting vanishing theta identities which will be discussed in Section
3.4
.

Blowup equations can even shed new light on the structure of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs itself. As mentioned earlier, it was found in
Gu:2020fem
that for the modularity of elliptic blowup equations for 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs to hold, the following constraint on tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
, gauge algebra
G
and matter representations
R
need to be satisfied:

\frac{h_{G}^{\vee}}{6}-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{12}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{G})=\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2}{2}.

(3.51)

Simply speaking, this constraint comes from the consistency requirement when converting the blowup equations of refined topological strings on non-compact Calabi-Yau threefolds to the elliptic form.
For twisted elliptic blowup equations, we find there exists similar constraints depending on the type of twists. For
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist with
n_{G}=2
, we find
3
3

3

The
\mathfrak{n}=1,\mathfrak{su}(2)+10\bf F
theory is not included.

\frac{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}}{6}-\frac{1}{24}\mathrm{Ind}(V^{frac}_{1/2})-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{12n^{G}_{i}}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{\mathring{G}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{4}-\frac{1}{4},&\quad\mathfrak{so}(2r)^{(2)},\\
\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{8}+\frac{1}{2},&\quad E_{6}^{(2)}.\end{array}\right.

(3.52)

Here
N_{i}
is the number of hypers
R_{i}^{\mathring{G}}
with KK-charge 0, with a twist coefficient
n^{G}_{i}
. We checked (
3.52
) is true for all
\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}(2N)^{(2)}
theories
4
4

4

Since
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{so}(2N-1)
, we have
\frac{2N-3}{6}-\frac{2}{24}-\frac{2N-8}{12}\times 2=\frac{3}{4}
.
,
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,3,4,G=\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theories,
\mathfrak{n}=2,G=\mathfrak{so}(10),\mathfrak{so}(12)
theories,
\mathfrak{n}=2,G=\mathfrak{su}(2N)
theories
5
5

5

Since
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(N)
, we have
\frac{N+1}{6}-\frac{2N-2}{24}-\frac{2N\times 1}{24}=\frac{1}{4}.
and
\mathfrak{n}=1,G=\mathfrak{su}(4)
theories.
For
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist, we have

\frac{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}}{6}-\frac{1}{18}\mathrm{Ind}(V^{frac}_{1/3})-\frac{N}{36}\mathrm{Ind}(R^{\mathring{G}})=\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{18}+\frac{1}{3}.

(3.53)

Here
N
is the number of hypers
R^{G_{2}}
with twist coefficient 3.
We checked (
3.53
) is true for
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,3,4,G=\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theories.
For
n^{G}=4
, we find

\frac{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}}{6}-\frac{1}{24}\mathrm{Ind}(V^{frac}_{1/2})-\frac{1}{48}\mathrm{Ind}(V^{frac}_{1/4})-\frac{N}{24}\mathrm{Ind}(R^{\mathring{G}})=\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{8}-\frac{1}{16}.

(3.54)

Here
N
is the number of fundamental hypers
R=\bf F
of
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(r)
. We checked (
3.54
) is true for
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,3,G=\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theories and
\mathfrak{n}=2,G=\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}
theories
6
6

6

Since
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(N)
, we have
\frac{N+1}{6}-\frac{2N-2}{24}-\frac{1}{48}-\frac{(2N+1)\times 1}{24}=\frac{3}{16}.
. Notice that these interesting constraints exactly give the
c
constants discussed in (
2.24
). In summary, denote
s
as all fractional KK charges, we have for all paired theories,

c=\frac{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}}{6}-\sum_{s}\zeta(-1,s)\mathrm{Ind}(V^{frac}_{s})-\sum_{i}\frac{N_{i}}{12r_{i}^{\rm out}}\mathrm{Ind}(R_{i}^{\mathring{G}})=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{2n_{G}}-\frac{1}{(n_{G})^{2}},\\
\frac{\mathfrak{n}}{2(n_{G})^{2}}+\frac{1}{n_{G}}.\end{array}\right.

(3.55)

Here
n_{G}=1,2,3,4
. The two possibilities here perhaps are related to the different types of multi-section Calabi-Yau geometries.

3.2
Modularity

The modularity of all elliptic blowup equations for rank-one 6d SCFTs has been proved in the Section 3.2 of
Gu:2020fem
. The modularity of twisted elliptic blowup equations actually inherits from its untwisted origin, that is the index of each part is directly reduced from its untwisted origin. For example, as we have mentioned, the modular index of twisted elliptic genera can be reduced from the modular index of untwisted ones. Therefore, we will be brief and only show one example here. Consider the pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=4
. From the general formula (
3.47
), we have the following unity twisted elliptic blowup equations for the twisted elliptic genera:

\displaystyle\sum_{\frac{1}{2}||\alpha^{\vee}||^{2}_{G_{2}}+d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}=d}

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\alpha^{\vee}|}\theta_{3}^{[a]}(4\tau,-4m_{\alpha^{\vee}}+(2-2||\alpha^{\vee}||^{2})(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})-4d^{\prime}\epsilon_{1}-4d^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle\times A_{{V}}^{G_{2}}(\tau,m,\alpha^{\vee})A^{\mathbf{7}_{1/3}\oplus\mathbf{7}_{2/3}}_{V}(\tau,m,\alpha^{\vee})

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}_{d^{\prime}}(\tau,m+\epsilon_{1}\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})\mathbb{E}_{d^{\prime\prime}}(\tau,m+\epsilon_{2}\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\theta_{3}^{[a]}(4\tau,2(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}))\mathbb{E}_{d}(\tau,m,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}),\qquad d=0,1,2,\ldots

Here the characteristic
a=0,\pm\frac{1}{4},\frac{1}{2}
.
Note the
B
field of the base
(-4)
-curve is even, which was already observed in
(
Kim:2020hhh
, Section 6.1)
.

We now show how to prove the modularity of twisted elliptic blowup equations (
3.2
). Denote
d_{0}=\frac{1}{2}||\alpha^{\vee}||^{2}_{G_{2}}
.
The classical part contributes to the index quadratic form as

\mathrm{Ind}_{\rm cl}=2(-\alpha^{\vee}\cdot m_{G_{2}}+(1/2-d_{0})(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})-d^{\prime}\epsilon_{1}-d^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{2})^{2}.

(3.56)

The
\Lambda
factor
\theta_{3}^{[a]}(4\tau,2(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}))
contributes index with
\mathrm{Ind}_{\Lambda}=\frac{1}{2}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})^{2}
.
The following identities for the coroot lattice of
G_{2}
are useful when computing the index from vector multiplets contribution:
\forall\alpha^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}_{G_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\in\Delta_{+}(G_{2})}(m\cdot\beta)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\beta)

\displaystyle=4m\cdot\alpha^{\vee},

(3.57)

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\in\Delta_{+}(G_{2})}(m\cdot\beta)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\beta)^{3}

\displaystyle=10d_{0}m\cdot\alpha^{\vee}=5(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\alpha^{\vee})(m\cdot\alpha^{\vee}),

(3.58)

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\in\Delta_{+}(G_{2})}(m\cdot\beta)^{2}(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\beta)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{10}{3}(m\cdot\alpha^{\vee})^{2}+\frac{5}{3}(m\cdot m)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\alpha^{\vee}).

(3.59)

Then by the definition in Appendix
B
, we find the
G_{2}
vector multiplet
A_{{V}}^{G_{2}}(\tau,m,\alpha^{\vee})
contributes to index as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{G_{2}}=

\displaystyle-\frac{5}{3}\left((\alpha^{\vee}\cdot m_{G_{2}})^{2}+d_{0}(m_{G_{2}}\cdot m_{G_{2}})\right)-\frac{10d_{0}-4}{3}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})\alpha^{\vee}\cdot m_{G_{2}}

(3.60)

\displaystyle-\frac{d_{0}}{3}\left(5d_{0}-2\right)(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}).

To compute the index contribution from the fractional vector multiplets, we still need the following useful formulas for
G_{2}
orbit
\mathcal{O}_{1,6}\subset\bf 7
:
\forall\alpha^{\vee}\in Q^{\vee}_{G_{2}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(m\cdot w)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot w)

\displaystyle=2m\cdot\alpha^{\vee},

(3.61)

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(m\cdot w)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot w)^{3}

\displaystyle=2d_{0}m\cdot\alpha^{\vee}=(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\alpha^{\vee})(m\cdot\alpha^{\vee}),

(3.62)

\displaystyle\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(m\cdot w)^{2}(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot w)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{3}(m\cdot\alpha^{\vee})^{2}+\frac{1}{3}(m\cdot m)(\alpha^{\vee}\cdot\alpha^{\vee}).

(3.63)

Then by the definition in Appendix
B
, we find the KK charge
1/3
and
2/3
parts
A^{\mathbf{7}_{1/3}\oplus\mathbf{7}_{2/3}}_{V}(\tau,m,\alpha^{\vee})
contribute to index as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathbf{7}_{1/3}\oplus\mathbf{7}_{2/3}}_{V}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Big((\alpha^{\vee}\cdot m_{G_{2}})^{2}+d_{0}(m_{G_{2}}\cdot m_{G_{2}})\Big)-\frac{2d_{0}-2}{3}(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})\alpha^{\vee}\cdot m_{G_{2}}

(3.64)

\displaystyle-\frac{d_{0}(d_{0}-1)}{3}(\epsilon_{1}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}+\epsilon_{2}^{2}).

From (
2.44
) we know the
d
-string twisted elliptic genus has index

\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{E}}(d,m_{G_{2}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})=-d({\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2}})^{2}+(2d^{2}-d)\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}-2dm_{G_{2}}\cdot m_{G_{2}}.

(3.65)

All together, we checked that given
d=d_{0}+d^{\prime}+d^{\prime\prime}
, the modularity of twisted elliptic blowup equation (
3.2
) holds, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ind}_{\rm cl}+\mathrm{Ind}_{V}^{G_{2}}+\mathrm{Ind}^{\mathbf{7}_{1/3}\oplus\mathbf{7}_{2/3}}_{V}+\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{E}}(d^{\prime},m_{G_{2}}+\epsilon_{1}\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})

(3.66)

\displaystyle+\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{E}}(d^{\prime\prime},m_{G_{2}}+\epsilon_{2}\alpha^{\vee},\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2})=\mathrm{Ind}_{\Lambda}+\mathrm{Ind}_{\mathbb{E}}(d,m_{G_{2}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}).

3.3
Recursion formula for twisted elliptic genera

In general, for
\mathfrak{n}\geq 3
and paired matter content, there exist a sufficient number of unity blowup equations which together give a recursion formula for the elliptic genera with respect to the string number
d
. For the untwisted situation, this has been discussed extensively in Section 4.1 of
Gu:2020fem
. For the current twisted situation, the recursion formula is very similar. We omit the derivation and only state the results here. It should be noted that for
\mathfrak{n}=1,2
, although there is no explicit recursion formula, the elliptic genera still can be solved for many theories, see Section 4.2 of
Gu:2020fem
. For simplicity, in this subsection, we first discuss the four pure gauge twisted theories, then move to the theories with matters.

For the pure gauge twisted theories
G^{(n)}=\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)},\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)},\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)},E_{6}^{(2)}
,
let us use the following shorthand notation for the classical term in blowup equations:

\theta_{i,\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{[a]}=\theta^{[a]}_{i}\left(\mathfrak{n}\tau,-\mathfrak{n}m_{\alpha^{\vee}}+(\mathfrak{n}-2)(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})-\mathfrak{n}((d_{0}+d_{1})\epsilon_{1}+(d_{0}+d_{2})\epsilon_{2})\right)\,,

(3.67)

where
m_{\alpha^{\vee}}={m}\cdot\alpha^{\vee}
and
m
is the gauge fugacity for
\mathring{G}
. Furthermore, we define

D_{d}=\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{1}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{1}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,0\}}^{[a_{1}]}\\
\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{2}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{2}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,0\}}^{[a_{2}]}\\
\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{3}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{3}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,0\}}^{[a_{3}]}\\
\end{array}\right),

(3.68)

as well as

D_{\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{\alpha^{\vee}}=\det\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{1}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{1}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{[a_{1}]}\\
\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{2}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{2}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{[a_{2}]}\\
\theta_{i,\{0,d,0\}}^{[a_{3}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{0,0,d\}}^{[a_{3}]}&\ \theta_{i,\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{[a_{3}]}\\
\end{array}\right).

(3.69)

Note that
D_{d}=D_{\{0,0,0\}}^{\alpha^{\vee}}
does not depend on

\alpha^{\vee}
.
Then the recursion formula of
d
-string twisted elliptic genera
\mathbb{E}_{d}(m,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
read

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{d}=\sum_{d_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}||\alpha^{\vee}||^{2},\,d_{1,2}<d}^{d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2}=d}

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\alpha^{\vee}|}\frac{D^{\alpha^{\vee}}_{\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}}{D_{d}}A_{V}^{\mathring{G}}(m,\alpha^{\vee})A_{V}^{frac}(m,\alpha^{\vee})

(3.70)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{E}_{d_{1}}(m+\epsilon_{1}{\alpha^{\vee}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})\mathbb{E}_{d_{2}}(m+\epsilon_{2}{\alpha^{\vee}},\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}).

Specializing to
d=1
, we obtain the following elegant formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genera of all four pure gauge twisted theories:

\mathbb{E}_{1}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta^{\vee}_{l}}\frac{D^{\alpha^{\vee}}_{\{1,0,0\}}}{D_{1}}\frac{\eta^{4}}{\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha})\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{1,2})\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}-2\epsilon_{+})}\prod_{\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\Delta\\
\alpha\cdot\beta=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}(m_{\beta})}\prod^{k}_{\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Delta_{s}\\
\alpha\cdot\gamma=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}^{[k]}(m_{\gamma})}.

(3.71)

Recall
\Delta_{s}
denotes the set of short roots, while
k
denotes all fractional KK-charges of
\bf F
appearing in the twist of adjoint representation. For example, for
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
,
k
takes values
1/3,2/3
.

For twisted theories with paired matters, we just need to add the hypermultiplet contribution to the above formulas. The classical term in (
3.67
) is replaced as

\theta_{i}^{[a]}(\mathfrak{n}\tau,-\mathfrak{n}m_{\alpha^{\vee}}^{\mathring{G}}+k_{\mathring{F}}m_{\lambda}^{\mathring{F}}+(y-\mathfrak{n}d_{0})(\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{2})-\mathfrak{n}d^{\prime}\epsilon_{1}-\mathfrak{n}d^{\prime\prime}\epsilon_{2}),

(3.72)

with a fixed unity
\lambda=\lambda_{\mathring{F}}
. Then
D_{d}
and
D_{\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}^{\alpha^{\vee}}
are defined the same with the pure gauge cases (
3.68
), (
3.69
). The universal recursion formula for twisted
d
-string elliptic genera
\mathbb{E}_{d}(m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2})
read

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{d}=\sum_{d_{0}=\tfrac{1}{2}||\alpha^{\vee}||^{2},\,d_{1,2}<d}^{d_{0}+d_{1}+d_{2}=d}

\displaystyle(-1)^{|\alpha^{\vee}|}\frac{D^{\alpha^{\vee}}_{\{d_{0},d_{1},d_{2}\}}}{D_{d}}A_{V}^{\mathring{G}}(m_{\mathring{G}},\alpha^{\vee})A_{V}^{frac}(m_{\mathring{G}},\alpha^{\vee})A_{{H}}^{\mathring{R}}(m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\alpha^{\vee},\lambda)

\displaystyle\ \times\mathbb{E}_{d_{1}}(m_{\mathring{G}}+\epsilon_{1}{\alpha^{\vee}},m_{\mathring{F}}+\epsilon_{1}\lambda,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{1})

\displaystyle\ \times\mathbb{E}_{d_{2}}(m_{\mathring{G}}+\epsilon_{2}{\alpha^{\vee}},m_{\mathring{F}}+\epsilon_{2}\lambda,\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{2}).

(3.73)

Specializing to
d=1
, we obtain the following universal formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genera:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}=\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta^{\vee}_{l}}

\displaystyle\frac{D^{\alpha^{\vee}}_{\{1,0,0\}}}{D_{1}}\frac{\eta^{4}}{\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha})\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{1,2})\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}-2\epsilon_{+})}

(3.74)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\beta\in\Delta\\
\alpha\cdot\beta=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}(m_{\beta})}\prod_{\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}\gamma\in\Delta_{s}\\
\alpha\cdot\gamma=1\end{subarray}}^{k}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}^{[k]}(m_{\gamma})}\prod_{\displaystyle\begin{subarray}{c}w\in R\\
\alpha\cdot w=1/2\end{subarray}}\frac{\theta_{1}^{[k^{\prime}]}(m_{w}+m_{f}+\epsilon_{+})}{\eta}.

Here
k^{\prime}
are the KK-charges of the twisted matter content, and the
w
are the weights of
\mathring{G}
and
m_{f}
are the flavor fugacities. We use this formula to compute the twisted one-string elliptic genera of lots of 6d twisted theories. In most cases, our results are new.

Though by the recursion formula, one can compute the elliptic genera to arbitrary number of strings, we are particular interested in the
reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera
defined by

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\rm red}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\theta_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{1})\theta_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{2})}{\eta(\tau)^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}).

(3.75)

The prefactor here comes from the well-known center of motion contribution in
\mathbb{C}_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}^{2}
, see e.g.
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
.
The simplicity of
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\rm red}
lies in that it only depends on
\epsilon_{+}
but not on
\epsilon_{-}
. Since there is no room for confusion, in the following we often omit the term “reduced”, and just say twisted one-string elliptic genera. The reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera exhibit many fascinating properties such as
\Gamma_{1}(N)
modularity and spectral flow symmetry, which will be discussed later.

3.4
Vanishing equations and vanishing theta identities

The vanishing elliptic blowup equations, although less constraining for elliptic genera than the unity ones, are still rather non-trivial functional equations. In particular, the leading degree of vanishing elliptic blowup equations does not involve contribution from elliptic genera, but only the classical and one-loop parts. Therefore, the leading degree equations usually give some interesting vanishing theta identities depending on the root and weight lattices of the 6d gauge algebras
G
, see many examples in

Gu:2019dan
;
Gu:2020fem
. In the twisted cases, we find many similar identities depending on the root and weight lattices of the truncated algebras
\mathring{G}
. In this subsection, we will explicitly show some leading degree identities for the vanishing twisted elliptic blowup equations given in Table
5
. We have checked these identities to sufficient high orders in the
q
expansions.

Let us first study the
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
cases, where
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{so}(7)
and the vanishing
\lambda_{\mathfrak{so}(7)}
can take value in the dominant orbit
\mathcal{O}_{1,6}
of vector representation
\bf 7
. For pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=4
, we derive the following leading degree vanishing theta identity:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{3}^{[a]}(4\tau,4m_{w})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{3})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.76)

Here
a=\pm 1/8,\pm 3/8
. It is easy to check for each
w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}
, the summand has index
\frac{4}{2}m_{w}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}m_{w}^{2}-\frac{5}{2}m_{w}^{2}=-m_{w}^{2}
which is invariant for the whole Weyl orbit. We checked this identity to high
q
orders. For
\mathfrak{n}=3
, we have one vector hypermultiplet with mass
m_{v}
and two spinor hypermultiplets with mass
m_{s}
. Note
\forall w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}
and
w^{\prime}\in\mathbf{8_{s}}
,
w\cdot w^{\prime}=\pm 1/2
. Thus the spinor hypermultiplets do not contribute to the leading degree vanishing theta identity. We then have

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{4}^{[a]}(3\tau,-3m_{w}+y)\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{w}+y)}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{3})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.77)

Here
y=m_{f}+\epsilon_{+}
and
a=0,\pm 1/3
. It is easy to check for each
w
, the summand has index
\frac{3}{2}(m_{w}-\frac{1}{3}y)^{2}+\frac{1}{2}(m_{w}+y)^{2}-\frac{1}{2}m_{w}^{2}-\frac{5}{2}m_{w}^{2}=-m_{w}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}y^{2}
which is invariant for the whole Weyl orbit. We also checked this identity for general
y
to high
q
orders. In fact, for 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=1,2,3,4
, we find the following unified formula for the leading degree vanishing theta identities:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,6}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{i}^{[a]}(\mathfrak{n}\tau,-\mathfrak{n}m_{w}+\sum y_{j})\prod_{j=1}^{4-\mathfrak{n}}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{w}+y_{j})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{3})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.78)

Recall
i=3
for even
n
and
i=4
for odd
n
.

Consider the
\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)^{(2)}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=4
, where
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{so}(2r+7)
and the vanishing
\lambda_{B_{r+3}}
can take value in the dominant orbit
\mathcal{O}_{1,2(r+3)}
of the vector representation. In these cases, the vector hypermultiplets can contribute to the leading degree of vanishing blowup equations. To be precise, we find the following leading degree vanishing theta identity for general
r
:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,2(r+3)}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{4}^{[a]}(4\tau,-4m_{w}+\sum y_{j})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{r+3})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}\prod_{j=1}^{2r}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{w}+y_{j})=0.

(3.79)

Here
a=\pm 1/8,\pm 3/8
and
y_{i}=m_{f_{i}}+\epsilon_{+}
.
We checked this identity for various
r
up to high
q
orders.
More
\mathfrak{so}
type examples with
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist include the
\mathfrak{so}(10)^{(2)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=2
, for which we find the following leading degree vanishing theta identities:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,8}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{3}^{[a]}(2\tau,-2m_{w}+\sum y_{i})\prod_{i=1}^{4}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{w}+y_{i})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{4})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.80)

Here
a=\pm 1/4
. Besides, for
\mathfrak{so}(12)^{(2)}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=2
, we find the following leading degree vanishing theta identities:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,10}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{3}^{[a]}(2\tau,-2m_{w}+\sum y_{i})\prod_{i=1}^{6}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{w}+y_{i})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(B_{5})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.81)

We checked all these identities for general
y_{i}
up to high
q
orders.

Now consider the
\mathfrak{su}({2r})^{(2)}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=2
where
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(r)
. First, for
\mathfrak{su}(2)^{(2)}
theory, the leading vanishing identity is trivial. For
\mathfrak{su}({4})^{(2)}
theory, the leading degree vanishing identity is for
C_{2}
:

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,4}}(-1)^{|w|}\frac{\theta_{3}^{[a]}(2\tau,-2m_{w})}{\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(C_{2})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0,

(3.82)

where
a=\pm 1/4
and
\mathcal{O}_{1,4}
is the dominant Weyl orbit in
\bf 5
. We checked this identity to high
q
orders. For arbitrary
r
, it is easy to find the leading degree vanishing theta identities for
C_{r}
can be uniformly written as

\sum_{w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,2r}}(-1)^{|w|}{\theta_{3}^{[a]}(2\tau,-2m_{w})}{\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta(C_{r})}^{w\cdot\alpha=1}\theta_{1}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}{\prod_{\beta\in\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}}^{w\cdot\beta=1}\theta_{4}(\tau,m_{\alpha})^{-1}}=0.

(3.83)

Again
a=\pm 1/4
and the
\mathcal{O}_{1,2r}
is the dominant Weyl orbit in the
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{r}
representation of
C_{r}
. Note each
w\in\mathcal{O}_{1,2r}
intersect with all weights of
\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}
by
r(r-1)/2
number of
1
,
r(r-1)/2
number of
-1
, while the rest intersection numbers are zero. We have checked identity (
3.83
) for
r=2,3,4,5
to high
q
orders.

4
Structure of twisted elliptic genera

We use the twisted elliptic blowup equations and the recursion formula (
3.71
) introduced in the last section to compute the twisted one-string elliptic genera of all twisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs. These data enable us to study some universal features of the twisted elliptic genera. Many of these features are the generalization of those of the ordinary
(0,4)
elliptic genera observed by del Zotto and Lockhart in
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
. According to the universal formula (
2.29
) and (
3.75
), the reduced twisted one-string R-R elliptic genera have the following form

\mathbb{E}_{1}(q,v,x,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})=q^{\frac{1}{6}-c}\Big(Z_{1}^{5d}(v,x,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})+\mathcal{O}(q^{1/n_{G}})\Big),

(4.84)

where
Z_{1}^{5d}
is the reduced one-instanton partition function of the 5d circle reduction theory.

4.1
Spectral flow symmetry

Spectral flow is a characteristic feature of 2d
\mathcal{N}=(2,2)
SCFTs, see e.g.
(
Blumenhagen:2009zz
, Chapter 5.4)
. The different sectors of 2d SCFTs are interpolated by a spectral flow parameter
\eta
. When
\eta\in\mathbb{Z}+\frac{1}{2}
, the spectral flow interpolates the NS sector with half-integer modes and the R sector with integer modes. For chiral primary
|h,q\rangle
of
(2,2)
SCFT, the conformal weight
h
and charge
q
transform under spectral flow with general
\eta
as

h_{\eta}=h-\eta q+\frac{\eta^{2}}{6}c^{\prime},\qquad q_{\eta}=q-\frac{c^{\prime}}{3}\eta.

(4.85)

Here the
c^{\prime}
is the central charge of the 2d SCFT.
We are interested in the
\eta=\frac{1}{2}
case. Using
h=q/2
, the spectral flow induces transformation

\Big|\frac{q}{2},q\Big\rangle_{\rm NS}\rightarrow\Big|\frac{c^{\prime}}{24},q-\frac{c^{\prime}}{6}\Big\rangle_{\rm R}.

(4.86)

Note the weight
\frac{c^{\prime}}{24}
indicates the ground state of the R sector. This phenomenon suggests an interesting transformation between NS-R elliptic genera and R-R elliptic genera.

Similar phenomena appear in 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs as well
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
.
Let us focus on the 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs of
one
BPS string in 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, possibly with twisted compactification.
In general, the spectral flow from R-R sector to NS-R sector for the
same
6d
(1,0)
theory possibly with a twist is induced by the following transformation:

\boxed{\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{NS-R}}^{\mathring{R}_{\mathrm{KK}}}\Big(q,v\Big)=\pm\Big(\frac{q^{1/4}}{v}\Big)^{\mathfrak{n}-h_{G}^{\vee}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{R-R}}^{\mathring{R}_{\mathrm{KK}}}\Big(q,\frac{q^{1/2}}{v}\Big).}

(4.87)

On the other hand, from the viewpoint of
(0,4)
NLSM, the NS-R elliptic genera are obtained by imposing anti-periodicity on the 2d chiral fermions.
This suggests the following elegant phenomenon for the twisted one-string elliptic genera: the spectral flow transformation shifts the KK charges of all hypermultiplets by half. To be precise, we find that for twisted one-string elliptic genera of different sectors:

\boxed{\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{NS-R}}^{\mathring{R}_{\mathrm{KK}\phantom{+1}}}(q,v)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{R-R}}^{\mathring{R}_{\mathrm{KK}+1/2}}(q,v).}

(4.88)

Note this is even true for the untwisted cases, which explains the NS-R elliptic genera in
DelZotto:2018tcj
have simple low energy behaviors as 5d pure gauge theories. With this understanding, we know that the NS-R elliptic genera have similar expansion as (
4.84
), as long as both the
c
and
Z_{1}^{5d}
are derived from the twisted matter content with all KK-charges shifted by half.
When the twisted matter content is invariant under KK-charge shift by half,
7
7

7

For pure gauge theories, this is automatically satisfied.
the above two properties induce a nontrivial symmetry for the R-R elliptic genus itself, i.e.,

\boxed{\mathbb{E}_{1}\Big(q,\frac{q^{1/2}}{v}\Big)=\Big(-\frac{q^{1/4}}{v}\Big)^{\mathfrak{n}-h_{G}^{\vee}}\,\mathbb{E}_{1}(q,v).}

(4.89)

We have checked this symmetry for all admissible twisted theories. The prefactor on the right hand side does not involve any information on the twist.
This means that
the spectral flow symmetry is preserved upon twisted compactification
.

4.2
The pure gauge cases

We first discuss the four pure gauge cases
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
,
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
,
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
and
E_{6}^{(2)}
.
Denote
\theta
and
f
as the weight generating the adjoint and fundamental representation respectively.
We find that for 6d
(1,0)
pure gauge
G^{(n)}
twisted theories with twist coefficient
N=2,3,4
, the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera have the following universal expansion

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{1}^{G^{(n)}}=q^{\frac{1}{6}-c}v^{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}-1}\bigg(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathring{G}}_{k\theta}v^{2n}+q^{{\frac{1}{N}}}(1+v^{2})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathring{G}}_{k\theta+f}v^{2n}+O(q^{\frac{2}{N}})\bigg).

(4.90)

For the
N=2
cases, we further find that the
q^{1}
term in the above expansion is
\chi^{\mathring{G}}_{2f}+\chi^{\mathring{G}}_{\theta}+2+\mathcal{O}(v^{2})
.
We also find the following interesting
spectral flow symmetry
:

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{G^{(n)}}\Big(q,\frac{q^{1/2}}{v}\Big)=\Big(-\frac{q^{1/2}}{v^{2}}\Big)^{\mathfrak{n}-3}\,\mathbb{E}_{1}^{G^{(n)}}(q,v),

(4.91)

as a special case of (
4.89
). This resembles the spectral flow symmetry for the reduced one-string elliptic genera associated to untwisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs studied in
DelZotto:2016pvm
.

4.2.1
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

The 6d
(1,0)
pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
SCFT enjoys a series of fascinating 2d quiver constructions for the worldsheet theories of the BPS strings such that the elliptic genera are exactly computable to arbitrary number of strings
Kim:2018gjo
. The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted circle compactification gives the 5d
\mathfrak{su}(3)
gauge theory with Chern-Simons level 9
Jefferson:2017ahm
. The blowup equations for this twisted theory have been studied before in
(
Kim:2020hhh
, Section 3.2.3)
, which are consistent with our universal elliptic form. As we have reviewed, this twisted theory can be Higgsed from 6d
(1,0)

G_{2}+\bf F
theory
Kim:2021cua
, thus the twisted elliptic genera are also exactly computable to arbitrary number of strings. Here we provide a new observation that is the spectral flow symmetry of its twisted one-string elliptic genus.

For
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
, the low energy gauge algebra is
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{su}(2)
.
Let us denote the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus as

\mathbb{E}_{1}=q^{-\frac{7}{48}}\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}q^{n}g_{n}\left(v,m_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\right)=q^{-\frac{7}{48}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{i}v^{-2i+j},\qquad 4n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.92)

We calculate
g_{n}
up to
n=4
from the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations with characteristics
a=0,\pm 1/3
. The results are in perfect agreement with the computations in
Kim:2020hhh
;
Kim:2021cua
;
Hayashi:2020hhb
. For example, when turning off the
\mathfrak{su}(2)
fugacity, we have

\displaystyle g_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{v(v^{2}+1)}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{\frac{1}{4}}=\frac{2v(v^{2}+1)}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{\frac{1}{2}}=\frac{(v^{6}+2v^{4}+2v^{2}+1)}{v\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},

(4.93)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2(v^{6}+2v^{4}+2v^{2}+1)}{v\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{1}=\frac{\left(4v^{6}+9v^{4}+9v^{2}+4\right)}{v\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}}.

(4.94)

With the
\mathfrak{su}(2)
fugacity, we find

\displaystyle g_{0}

\displaystyle=v\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{A_{1}}_{n\theta}v^{2n}=v+\mathbf{3}v^{3}+\mathbf{5}v^{5}+\mathbf{7}v^{7}+\dots,

(4.95)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{1}{4}}

\displaystyle=v(1+v^{2})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{A_{1}}_{f+n\theta}v^{2n}=\mathbf{2}v+(\mathbf{2}+\mathbf{4})v^{3}+(\mathbf{4}+\mathbf{6})v^{5}+(\mathbf{6}+\mathbf{8})v^{7}+\dots,

(4.96)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=v^{-1}(1+v^{2}+v^{4})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{A_{1}}_{n\theta}v^{2n}=v^{-1}+(\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{3})v+(\mathbf{1}+\mathbf{3}+\mathbf{5})v^{3}+\dots,

(4.97)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle=v^{-1}(1+2v^{2}+2v^{4}+v^{6})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{A_{1}}_{f+n\theta}v^{2n}=\mathbf{2}v^{-1}+(2\times\mathbf{2}+\mathbf{4})v+\dots.

(4.98)

The
g_{0}
result shows that the leading
q
order of the twisted one-string elliptic genus gives the one
\mathfrak{su}(2)
instanton Hilbert series
Benvenuti:2010pq
, which is expected since the circle reduction of the 6d
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory should be a pure
\mathfrak{su}(2)
theory with
\theta
angle
0
. We also have the following Table
6
for the coefficients
b_{ij}
. The manifest symmetry of the coefficient matrix suggests the following spectral flow symmetry of the twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}}(q,{q^{1/2}}/{v})=\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}}(q,v).

(4.99)

This resembles the spectral flow symmetry of the one-string elliptic genus of untwisted 6d
\mathfrak{su}(3)
theory observed in
DelZotto:2016pvm
. It is actually easy to prove this identity from the exact formula of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
in (
2.38
).

Table 6:
The coefficient matrix
b_{ij}
for the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
of 6d pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory.

Interestingly, we notice that the twisted elliptic blowup equations for
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
allow another solution for the twisted one-string elliptic genera if we set the characteristic
a=1/2,\pm 1/6
. We call this as the
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
case, as the low energy limit gives the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
theory. In this case, combining together the twisted elliptic blowup equations and modular ansatz which will be discussed later, we solve the twisted one-string elliptic genus with the
\mathfrak{sp}(1)
fugacity turned off. Let us denote the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus as

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\pi}(q,v)=q^{-\frac{7}{48}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}q^{n}g_{n}^{\pi}\left(v,m_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\right)=q^{-\frac{7}{48}}\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}^{\pi}q^{i}v^{-2i+j},\qquad 4n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.100)

We calculate
g_{n}^{\pi}
up to
n=4
. For example,

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{2v^{2}}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{\frac{1}{4}}^{\pi}=\frac{4v^{2}}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{\frac{1}{2}}^{\pi}=\frac{6v^{2}}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},

(4.101)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{3}{4}}^{\pi}

\displaystyle=\frac{12v^{2}}{\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}},\qquad g_{1}^{\pi}=\frac{2(v^{8}+11v^{4}+1)}{v^{2}\left(v^{2}-1\right)^{2}}.

With the gauge
\mathfrak{su}(2)
fugacity, we find

\displaystyle g_{0}^{\pi}=v^{2}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{n\theta+f}v^{2n}=\mathbf{2}v^{2}+\mathbf{4}v^{4}+\mathbf{6}v^{6}+\dots.

(4.102)

This shows the leading
q
order of twisted elliptic genus
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\pi}
is indeed the 5d one
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
instanton partition function. We also have the Table
7
for the coefficients
b_{ij}^{\pi}
. The manifest symmetry of the coefficient matrix shows that the solution
\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\pi}
also satisfies the spectral flow symmetry (
4.99
). We regard this as further evidence that this serves as a good elliptic completion of 5d one
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
instanton partition function.
However, to push forward to two-strings, we meet some inconsistency when solving the blowup equations. It is not clear to us whether there would exist a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of the two-string elliptic genus of 6d
(1,0)
pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
theory that has low energy limit as 5d two
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
instantons.

4.2.2
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted circle compactification of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(8)
SCFT gives a non-Lagrangian 5d KK theory. The twisted elliptic genera can be exactly computed to an arbitrary number of strings by 2d localization
Kim:2021cua
. The unity blowup equations for this twisted theory have been studied before in
(
Kim:2020hhh
, Section 6.2)
, which are consistent with our elliptic form.
Using our universal
\mathbb{E}_{1}
formula (
3.71
), we calculate the twisted one-string elliptic genus for this
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theory and find it in complete agreement with the 2d localization results. Let us denote the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus of
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
as

\mathbb{E}_{1}=q^{-\frac{7}{12}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{n}g_{n}(v,m_{\mathfrak{so}(7)})=q^{-\frac{7}{12}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j},\qquad 2n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.103)

When turning off the
\mathfrak{so}(7)
fugacities, we obtain

\displaystyle g_{0}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{v^{4}\left(v^{8}+13v^{6}+28v^{4}+13v^{2}+1\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{8}},

(4.104)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{1}{2}}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{7v^{4}\left(v^{2}+1\right)^{2}\left(v^{4}+6v^{2}+1\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{8}},

(4.105)

\displaystyle g_{1}=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2v^{4}\left(25v^{8}+189v^{6}+300v^{4}+189v^{2}+25\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{8}}.

(4.106)

With the
\mathfrak{so}(7)
gauge fugacities, we have

g_{0}=v^{4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi_{n\theta}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}v^{2n}=v^{4}+\mathbf{21}v^{6}+\mathbf{168}v^{8}+\mathbf{825}v^{10}+\mathbf{3003}v^{12}+\dots.

(4.107)

This is exactly the one
\mathfrak{so}(7)
instanton Hilbert series
Benvenuti:2010pq
, thus agrees with the expectation that the circle reduction of the twisted theory should be a 5d pure
\mathfrak{so}(7)
gauge theory. Besides, we have

g_{\frac{1}{2}}=v^{4}(1+v^{2})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{f+n\theta}v^{2n}=\mathbf{7}v^{4}+({\mathbf{105}+\mathbf{7}})v^{6}+(\mathbf{798}+\mathbf{105})v^{8}+(\mathbf{3696}+\mathbf{798})v^{10}+\dots

and
g_{1}=(\mathbf{27}+\mathbf{21}+2)v^{4}+\dots
. We also have the Table
8
for the Fourier coefficients
b_{ij}
. The representations
\chi_{n\theta}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}
are colored in orange.
The manifest anti-symmetric coefficient matrix implies the following spectral flow symmetry

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}}(q,{q^{1/2}}/{v})=-q^{1/2}v^{-2}\,\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}}(q,v).

(4.108)

4.2.3
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twisted circle compactification of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(8)
SCFT gives the 5d
\mathfrak{su}(4)
gauge theory with Chern-Simons level 8
Jefferson:2017ahm
. The circle reduction on the other hand gives a 5d pure
G_{2}
gauge theory. The worldsheet theories of the BPS strings for 6d
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory do not have 2d quiver descriptions, thus the computation of elliptic genera is quite nontrivial. The blowup equations for this twisted theory have been studied before in
(
Kim:2020hhh
, Section 6.1)
, which are consistent with our elliptic form. There are in total four unity blowup equations and no vanishing one. Let us denote the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus as

\mathbb{E}_{1}=q^{-\frac{7}{18}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{n}g_{n}(v,m_{G_{2}})=q^{-\frac{7}{18}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j},\qquad 3n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.109)

From our universal
\mathbb{E}_{1}
formula (
3.71
), we calculate
g_{n}
up to
n=4
. For example, turning off the
G_{2}
fugacities, we have

\displaystyle g_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{9}+8v^{7}+8v^{5}+v^{3}}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{6}}=v^{3}+14v^{5}+77v^{7}+273v^{9}+\dots,

(4.110)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{1}{3}}=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{3}\left(v^{2}+1\right)\left(7v^{4}+22v^{2}+7\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{6}}=7v^{3}+71v^{5}+350v^{7}+\dots,

\displaystyle g_{\frac{2}{3}}=

\displaystyle\frac{7v^{3}\left(v^{2}+1\right)\left(5v^{4}+8v^{2}+5\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{6}}=35v^{3}+301v^{5}+1372v^{7}+\dots.

Keeping the
G_{2}
gauge fugacities, we find the following exact formulas by the infinite summations of
G_{2}
characters

\displaystyle g_{0}

\displaystyle=v^{3}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi_{n\theta}^{G_{2}}v^{2n},\qquad\quad g_{\frac{1}{3}}=v^{3}(1+v^{2})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta}v^{2n},

\displaystyle g_{\frac{2}{3}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\chi^{G_{2}}_{2f+n\theta}+\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta}+\chi^{G_{2}}_{n\theta})v^{2n+3}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(\chi^{G_{2}}_{2f+n\theta}+\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta})v^{2n+5}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{G_{2}}_{2f+n\theta}v^{2n+7}.

The
g_{0}
formula shows that the circle reduction of 6d
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory is indeed a 5d pure
G_{2}
theory.
We also have the Table
9
for the coefficients
b_{ij}
,
i,j=0,1,2,\dots
, where the coefficients
\chi_{n\theta}^{G_{2}}
in
g_{0}
in colored orange. From the obvious anti-symmetry coefficient matrix, we find the following spectral flow symmetry

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}}(q,{q^{1/2}}/{v})=-q^{1/2}v^{-2}\,\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}}(q,v).

(4.111)

4.2.4
E_{6}^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted circle compactification of 6d
(1,0)

E_{6}
SCFT gives a non-Lagrangian 5d KK theory, while the circle reduction gives a 5d pure
F_{4}
gauge theory. This twisted theory has six unity blowup equations and no vanishing one.
The twisted elliptic genera of this theory have not been computed before.
We use the universal
\mathbb{E}_{1}
formula (
3.71
) to compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus. Denote the reduced twisted elliptic genus as

\mathbb{E}_{1}=q^{-\frac{13}{12}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{n}g_{n}(v,m_{F_{4}})=q^{-\frac{13}{12}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j},\qquad 2n\in\mathbb{Z}.

(4.112)

Turning off the
F_{4}
fugacities, we obtain

\displaystyle g_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{8}(v^{16}+36v^{14}+341v^{12}+1208v^{10}+1820v^{8}+1208v^{6}+341v^{4}+36v^{2}+1)}{(1-v^{2})^{16}},

(4.113)

\displaystyle g_{\frac{1}{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{13v^{8}\left(v^{2}+1\right)^{2}\left(2v^{12}+47v^{10}+274v^{8}+506v^{6}+274v^{4}+47v^{2}+2\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{16}},

\displaystyle g_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{2v^{8}}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{16}}\big(189v^{16}+3931v^{14}+24233v^{12}+64761v^{10}+88332v^{8}+64761v^{6}

\displaystyle+24233v^{4}+3931v^{2}+189\big).

When keeping the
F_{4}
fugacities, we have

g_{0}=v^{8}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi_{n\theta}^{F_{4}}v^{2n}=v^{8}+\mathbf{52}v^{10}+\mathbf{1053}v^{12}+\mathbf{12376}v^{14}+\dots.

(4.114)

This is exactly the one
F_{4}
instanton Hilbert series
Benvenuti:2010pq
, thus agrees with the expectation that the circle dimension reduction should be a 5d pure
F_{4}
gauge theory. Besides, we find

g_{\frac{1}{2}}=v^{8}(1+v^{2})\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{F_{4}}_{n\theta+f}v^{2n}=\mathbf{26}v^{8}+({\mathbf{1053}+\mathbf{26}})v^{10}+(\mathbf{17901}+\mathbf{1053})v^{12}+\dots

(4.115)

and
g_{1}=(\mathbf{324}+\mathbf{52}+2)v^{8}+\dots
.
We also have the Table
10
for the coefficients
b_{ij}
. From the anti-symmetric coefficient matrix, we observe the following spectral flow symmetry

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{E_{6}^{(2)}}(q,{q^{1/2}}/{v})=-q^{3/2}v^{-6}\,\mathbb{E}_{1}^{E_{6}^{(2)}}(q,v).

(4.116)

4.3
Universal features with matters

In this section, we discuss the universal features of the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera for twisted 6d theories with matters. Most of our observations are the twisted generalization of those in
DelZotto:2018tcj
. These include the spectral flow symmetry and the precise spectrum of the twisted R-R vacuum, NS-R vacuum and possibly the excited states with fractional KK-charges above the NS-R vacuum. For all reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera we solved from blowup equations, we analyze these features in the
(q,v)
expansion:

\mathbb{E}_{1}(q,v)=q^{\frac{1}{6}-c}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}c_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{j}=q^{\frac{1}{6}-c}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j},\qquad Ni,j\in\mathbb{Z},

(4.117)

where
N
is twist coefficient. All coefficients
c_{ij}
should be integers as they represent the states in the 2d worldsheet theories, in particular form representations of
\mathring{G}
and
\mathring{F}
when turning on the fugacities. The
b_{ij}
coefficients are slightly shifted from the
c_{ij}
coefficients.
As we have seen in the pure gauge cases, the
b_{ij}
coefficients are very useful to show explicitly the spectral flow symmetry. Therefore, we will show the
b_{ij}
coefficient matrix for all examples in the following.

4.3.1
\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}{(2r+8)}^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}{(2r+8)}+2r\bf V
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}
and twisted matter content
2r\mathbf{V}_{0}
. The circle reduction gives a 5d
\mathcal{N}=1

\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}+2r\bf F
theory whose 5d Nekrasov partition function is exactly computable by localization. On the other hand, the circle reduction of the NS-R sector gives a 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}
theory. For all
\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}{(2r+8)}^{(2)}
theories with
r\geq 0
, we use the recursion formula (
3.74
) to compute the one-string elliptic genera and find complete agreement with the 2d localization formulas.
Let us study the following expansion for the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+8)}^{(2)}}(q,v)=q^{-\frac{7}{12}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}c_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{j}=q^{-\frac{7}{12}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j}.

(4.118)

We observe the following patterns for the Fourier coefficients
c_{ij}
:

•

c_{0,j}=0
for
j<2r+4
. This is consistent with the fact that the reduced one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}+2r\bf F
theory has
v
expansion starting from
v^{2r+4}
. The
v
expansion coefficients are colored orange in the Tables
11
,
12
and
13
.

•

c_{\frac{3}{2}+n,-2-2n}=\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{n\theta}
and
c_{2+n,-3-2n}=0
. This shows that the leading
q
order of the NS-R twisted elliptic genera indeed gives the 5d one
\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}
instanton Hilbert series
Benvenuti:2010pq
. The coefficients are colored red in the Tables
11
,
12
and
13
.

•

c_{\frac{5}{2}+n,-1-2n}=-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{f+n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}{(2r)}}_{f}
. These are the first excited states with integer modes and a half KK-charge above the NS-R vacuum. The coefficients are colored blue in the Tables
11
,
12
and
13
.

•

c_{2+n,-2-2n}=\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{f+n\theta}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{f+(n-1)\theta}
. These are the first excited states with half-integer modes and a half KK-charge above the NS-R vacuum. The coefficients are colored cyan in the Tables
11
,
12
and
13
.

•

c_{2+n,-1-2n}=-\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}{(2r)}}_{f}
. These are the second excited states with half-integer modes and KK-charge 1 above the NS-R vacuum.

•

c_{\frac{5}{2},-2}=\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{\theta}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}{(2r+7)}}_{2f}+\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}{(2r)}}_{2f}+2
. These are the second excited states with integer modes and KK-charge 1 above the NS-R vacuum. The coefficients are colored purple in the Tables
11
,
12
and
13
.

\mathfrak{n}=4
,
\mathfrak{so}(10)^{(2)}

From the recursion formula (
3.74
), we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
4
and find complete agreement with the 2d quiver formula. In particular, we obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
11
. We checked that the coefficients satisfy all universal behaviors summarized earlier with
r=1
.

4.3.2
\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{su}(2r)+4r\bf F
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(r)
and twisted matter content
2r(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1/2})
which is invariant under the KK-charge shift by
1/2
. The circle reduction gives a 5d
\mathcal{N}=1

\mathfrak{sp}(r)+2r\bf F
theory whose 5d Nekrasov partition function is exactly computable by localization. However, the twisted elliptic genera, albeit the simple look, do not to our knowledge have a 2d quiver gauge theory description. We utilize the blowup equations to compute the twisted one-string elliptic genera. For arbitrary
r\geq 1
, the twisted elliptic blowup equations can have two possibilities for the characteristics
a=\pm 1/4
.
From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we solve the one-string twisted elliptic genera for
r=1,2,3,4
. We find the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus has the following spectral flow symmetry

\mathbb{E}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}}_{1}\Big(q,\frac{\sqrt{q}}{v}\Big)=-\Big(\frac{\sqrt{q}}{v^{2}}\Big)^{r-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}}_{1}(q,{v}).

(4.119)

We also find the following compact formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genus when turning off all gauge and flavor fugacities:

\mathbb{E}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{2^{2r-1}\eta(\tau)^{8r-3}}{\eta(\frac{\tau}{2})^{4r-2}}\frac{\theta_{4}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}{(\theta_{2}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})\theta_{3}(\tau,\epsilon_{+}))^{2r}}.

(4.120)

It is easy to prove the spectral flow symmetry from this expression. In the following, we explicitly show the results for
r=1,2,3
.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(2)^{(2)}

From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we calculate the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. We can also use the 2d localization to compute the twisted elliptic genus in this case as the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist for the gauge algebra
\mathfrak{su}(2)
is trivial. We only need to fold the four fundamentals. For one-string, we have

\mathbb{E}_{1}=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{2}\theta_{1}(a+\epsilon_{+}-m_{i})\theta_{4}(a+\epsilon_{+}+m_{i})}{\eta^{2}\theta_{1}(2a)\theta_{1}(2a+2\epsilon_{+})}+(a\rightarrow-a),

(4.121)

We checked this agrees with our results solved from blowup equations to high
q
orders. We also obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
14
. The spectral flow symmetry manifests as expected. We also checked the spectral flow symmetry with all gauge and flavor parameters turned on. Notice the leading orders of both R-R and NS-R elliptic genera give the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{su}(2)+2\bf F
. With gauge and flavor fugacities turned on, we have the following exact formula for the
v
expansion:

Z_{1}^{5d}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big(-\chi^{b}_{(1)}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{n\theta}v^{1+2n}+\chi^{a}_{(1)}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{f+n\theta}v^{2+2n}\Big).

(4.122)

Here the truncated flavor symmetry
\mathfrak{so}(4)\sim\mathfrak{su}(2)_{a}\times\mathfrak{su}(2)_{b}
.

of 6d

\mathfrak{n}=2

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(4)^{(2)}

From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. We obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
15
. The obvious anti-symmetric coefficient matrix shows the self-duality under spectral flow as expected. The leading orders of both R-R and NS-R elliptic genera should give the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{sp}(2)+4\bf F
. From ADHM construction, we obtain the following exact formula for the
v
expansion:

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)+4\bf F}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(8)}_{S}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{n\theta}v^{2+2n}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(8)}_{C}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{f+n\theta}v^{3+2n}\Big).

(4.123)

where
\mathfrak{so}(8)
is the pertubative 5d flavor symmetry. The Fourier coefficients completely agree with the orange numbers in Table
15
.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(6)^{(2)}

From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we calculate the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. We obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
16
. The obvious anti-symmetric coefficient matrix shows the self-duality under spectral flow. The leading orders of both R-R and NS-R elliptic genera give the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{sp}(3)+6\bf F
. From ADHM construction, we find the following exact formula for the
v
expansion:

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{sp}(3)+6\bf F}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(12)}_{S}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(3)}_{n\theta}v^{1+2n}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(12)}_{C}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(3)}_{f+n\theta}v^{2+2n}\Big).

(4.124)

where
\mathfrak{so}(12)
is the pertubative 5d flavor symmetry. We checked that these coefficients completely agree with the orange numbers in Table
16
.

4.3.3
\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)+2(2r+1)\bf F
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(r)
and twisted matter content
(2r+1)(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1/2})
which is invariant under the KK-charge shift by
1/2
. The circle reduction gives 5d
\mathcal{N}=1

\mathfrak{sp}(r)+(2r+1)\bf F

gauge theories whose 5d Nekrasov partition function is exactly computable by localization. The twisted circle compactification gives 5d
\mathcal{N}=1

\mathfrak{so}(2r+3)+(2r+1)\bf V
KK theories
Hayashi:2015vhy
. To our knowledge, there is no 2d quiver gauge theory description in the current situation. Therefore, it is important to use blowup equations to solve the twisted elliptic genera and study their properties. Indeed, we successfully solve the twisted one-string elliptic genera for
r=1,2,3,4
to high
q
orders. We find the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus has the following spectral flow symmetry

\mathbb{E}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}}_{1}\Big(q,\frac{\sqrt{q}}{v}\Big)=-\Big(\frac{{q}^{1/4}}{v}\Big)^{2r-1}\mathbb{E}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}}_{1}(q,{v}).

(4.125)

We also manage to find the following compact formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genus for arbitrary
r
:

\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\eta(\frac{\tau}{4})^{2}\theta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2},\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\frac{\tau}{2})^{2}\theta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2})}\Big(\frac{2\theta_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})\theta_{4}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\theta_{1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})\theta_{4}(\tau)}\Big)^{2r}.

(4.126)

We checked this formula for
r=1,2,3,4
against the twisted elliptic genera solved from blowup equations and found perfect agreement.
It is easy to prove the spectral flow symmetry from this expression.

It is interesting to remark that the twisted elliptic blowup equations also allow nice solutions for the twisted matter content
(2r+1)(\mathbf{F}_{1/4}+\mathbf{F}_{3/4})
. This choice of the KK-charges of the hypermultiplets
\bf F
has its merit in that they coincide with those of the
\bf F
in the twist of vector multiplet, i.e.
\mathbf{Adj}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1/4}+\mathbf{F}_{3/4}+\mathbf{\Lambda}^{2}_{1/2}
. This brings in some simplification that the
B
field of the tensor parameter becomes integral. In fact, there are two possibilities for such
B
field – even and odd, corresponding to two non-equivalent circle reductions to 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
\mathfrak{sp}(r)_{0}
or
\mathfrak{sp}(r)_{\pi}
theory. We leave the discussion for these situations in Appendix
C
.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted circle compactification of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{su}(3)+6\bf F
theory gives a 5d rank-3 KK theory which has gauge theory description as 5d
\mathfrak{sp}(2)+3\bf\Lambda^{2}
or 5d
\mathfrak{so}(5)+3\bf V
theory
Hayashi:2015vhy
.
From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. The coefficients
b_{ij}
are listed in Table
17
. The obvious anti-symmetric coefficient matrix shows the self-duality under spectral flow. For the low energy limit, the 5d one-instanton partition function of
\mathfrak{su}(2)+3\bf F
theory can be computed by localization as

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)+3\bf F}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(6)}_{S}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{n\theta}v^{1+2n}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(6)}_{C}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{f+n\theta}v^{2+2n}\Big).

(4.127)

where
\mathfrak{so}(6)
is the pertubative 5d flavor symmetry. It is easy to check that the
v
expansion coefficients are consistent with the orange numbers in Table
17
.

Other examples

\mathfrak{n}=4,E_{6}^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

E_{6}+2\bf F
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=F_{4}
and twisted matter content
\mathbf{26}_{0}+\mathbf{26}_{1/2}
which is invariant under the KK-charge shift by
1/2
. The twisted partition function has not been computed before.
From the recursion formula (
3.74
), we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. We summarize the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
19
. Notice the obvious anti-symmetric coefficient matrix, this shows that the twisted theory has self-dual spectral flow symmetry, albeit the nontrivial matter content. The leading
q
order of
\mathbb{E}_{1}
should give the one-instanton Nekrasov partition function of 5d
F_{4}+\bf F
theory, whose exact
v
expansion formula can be found in
(
DelZotto:2018tcj
, Equation (H.36))
. For example, when turning off all fugacities,

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{F_{4}+\bf F}(v)=

\displaystyle\frac{v^{7}}{(v-1)^{10}(v+1)^{16}}(v^{12}+10v^{11}-49v^{10}+266v^{9}-549v^{8}+1068v^{7}

(4.129)

\displaystyle-1110v^{6}+1068v^{5}-549v^{4}+266v^{3}-49v^{2}+10v+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v^{7}+4v^{8}-78v^{9}+754v^{10}-4433v^{11}+21060v^{12}+\dots

We have checked they are in complete agreement. The
v
expansion coefficients of 5d
Z_{1}^{F_{4}+\bf F}
are colored in orange in Table
19
.

Table 20:
The coefficient matrix
b_{ij}
for the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
of 6d
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}

We find the leading
q
order of the R-R sector, i.e., the orange coefficients gives the one-instanton Nekrasov partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf V+S
theory, while the leading
q
order of the NS-R sector, i.e., the red coefficients gives the one of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf S
theory. For 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf S
theory, the ADHM description has been studied in
Kim:2018gjo
. For example, the reduced one-instanton partition function is given by

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf S}(u_{i},m)=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\frac{\sinh(4\epsilon_{+}-2u_{i})\sinh(m\pm(u_{i}-\epsilon_{+}))}{\prod_{j(\neq i)}\sinh(u_{ij})\sinh(2\epsilon_{+}-u_{ij})\sinh(2\epsilon_{+}-u_{i}-u_{j})}.

(4.130)

Here
u_{i}
satisfying
u_{1}+u_{2}+u_{3}+u_{4}=0
are the fugacities of
\mathfrak{su}(4)
embedded in
\mathfrak{so}(7)
.
From this formula, we find

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf S}(v)=\frac{2v^{4}\left(v^{6}-2v^{5}+8v^{4}-6v^{3}+8v^{2}-2v+1\right)}{(v-1)^{6}(v+1)^{8}}.

(4.131)

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf S}(v,m_{\mathfrak{so}(7)},m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)})=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)}v^{2n+4}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta+s}v^{2n+5}.

(4.132)

This perfectly agrees with the leading
q
order of the NS-R twisted elliptic genus. For
\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf V+S
theory, we can compare with the reduced one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+{\bf V}+4\bf S
theory given in (H.15) of
DelZotto:2018tcj
. After decoupling three
\bf S
, we find the resulting 5d partition function is

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf V+S}(v,m_{\mathfrak{so}(7)},m_{v},m_{s})=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)_{v}}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)_{s}}v^{2n+4}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta+v+s}v^{2n+6}

(4.133)

\displaystyle-\left(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta+v}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)_{s}}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta+s}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)_{v}}\right)v^{2n+5}.

This perfectly agrees with the leading
q
order of the R-R twisted elliptic genus that is when turning off all gauge and flavor fugacities:

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf V+S}(v)=\frac{2v^{4}\left(2v^{2}-3v+2\right)}{(v-1)^{2}(v+1)^{8}}.

(4.134)

\mathfrak{n}=3,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(8)+\bf V+S+C
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=G_{2}
and twisted matter content
\mathbf{7}_{0}+\mathbf{7}_{1/3}+\mathbf{7}_{2/3}
.
From the recursion formula (
3.71
), we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
11/3
. In particular, we obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
21
. We observe the leading
q
order of R-R elliptic genus gives the one-instanton partition function of 5d
G_{2}+\bf F
theory
DelZotto:2018tcj
:

Z_{1}^{G_{2}+\bf F}=\sum_{n=0}\chi^{G_{2}}_{n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)}v^{2n+3}-\sum_{n=0}\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta}v^{2n+4}.

(4.135)

Besides, the leading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored red has coefficients
\chi^{G_{2}}_{n\theta}
as the one
G_{2}
instanton Hilbert series. The subleading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored blue has coefficients
-\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)}
, which are the excited states over NS-R vacuum with KK-charge
1/3
. The subsubleading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored cyan has coefficients
(\chi^{G_{2}}_{f}+\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(2)})\chi^{G_{2}}_{n\theta}
, which are the excited states over NS-R vacuum with KK-charge
2/3
.

Table 22:
The coefficient matrix
b_{ij}
for the
\mathbb{E}_{1}
of 6d
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=2
.

Our results are consistent with the 5d limits in both R-R and NS-R sectors. The leading
q
order of the NS-R sector gives the 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+2\bf S
theory whose 5d partition function can be computed by localization
Kim:2018gjo
or by 5d blowup equations
Kim:2019uqw
. We use the localization formula in
Kim:2018gjo
to compute the 5d one-instanton partition function as

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+2\bf S}=\frac{v^{4}\left(5v^{4}-12v^{3}+22v^{2}-12v+5\right)}{(v-1)^{4}(v+1)^{8}}=5v^{4}-32v^{5}+140v^{6}-448v^{7}+\dots.

(4.136)

One can see the coefficients are in agreement with the red numbers in Table
22
. One can also turn on the gauge and flavor fugacities, in which cases the
Z_{1}
formula as
v
expansion can be found in
(
DelZotto:2018tcj
, Equation (H.27))
.
The leading
q
order of the R-R sector should give the 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+2\mathbf{V}+2\bf S
theory whose partition function to our knowledge has not been computed before. Luckily we can compare with the reduced one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(7)+2{\bf V}+6\bf S
theory given in
(
Gu:2020fem
, Equation (E.2))
. After decoupling four
\bf S
, we find the resulting 5d partition function is

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+2\mathbf{V}+2\mathbf{S}}=

\displaystyle v^{2}+\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{v}}v^{3}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(100)}v^{4}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{v}}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{s}}v^{2n+4}

(4.137)

\displaystyle-\left(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(1n0)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{v}}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{s}}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(0n1)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{v}}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{s}}\right)v^{2n+5}

\displaystyle+\left(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(2n0)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{s}}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(1n1)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{v}}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{s}}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(0n2)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(01)_{v}}\right)v^{2n+6}

\displaystyle-\left(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(2n1)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{s}}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(1n2)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)_{v}}\right)v^{2n+7}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(2n2)}v^{2n+8}.

We checked that this completely agrees with leading
q
order of the R-R elliptic genus up to an overall minus sign:

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)+2\mathbf{V}+2\mathbf{S}}(v)=\frac{v^{2}\left(v^{4}+12v^{3}+78v^{2}+12v+1\right)}{(v+1)^{8}}=v^{2}+4v^{3}+18v^{4}-300v^{5}+\dots

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(8)+2(\bf V+S+C)
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=G_{2}
and twisted matter content
2(\mathbf{7}_{0}+\mathbf{7}_{1/3}+\mathbf{7}_{2/3})
. From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genera
\mathbb{E}_{1}
to the order
q^{16/3}
. We summarize the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
23
. The leading
q
order of the R-R elliptic genus should give the 5d partition function of
G_{2}+2\bf F
theory. We compute the 5d one-instanton partition function of
G_{2}+2\bf F
theory from the localization formula in
Kim:2018gjo
as

Z_{1}^{G_{2}+2\bf F}=-\frac{v^{3}\left(5v^{6}-28v^{5}+67v^{4}-88v^{3}+67v^{2}-28v+5\right)}{\left(1-v^{2}\right)^{6}}.

(4.138)

We checked that the Fourier coefficients here are in agreement with the orange numbers in Table
23
.
The leading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored red has coefficients
\chi^{G_{2}}_{n\theta}
as expected, since the circle reduction of the NS-R sector should be a 5d pure
G_{2}
theory. We find the subleading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored blue has coefficients
-\chi^{G_{2}}_{f+n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}_{(10)}
, which represent the first excited states with KK-charge
1/3
above the NS-R vacuum.
Interestingly, we notice the diagonal elements in Table
23
are always zero, but we could not find an explanation for it.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{so}(10)^{(2)}

The
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{so}(10)+4{\bf V}+2\bf S
theory has low energy gauge algebra
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{so}(9)
and twisted matter content
4\mathbf{V}_{0}+\mathbf{S}_{0}+\mathbf{S}_{1/2}
. From the unity twisted elliptic blowup equations, we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus
\mathbb{E}_{1}
to
q^{7}
order. In particular, we obtain the Fourier coefficients
b_{ij}
in the
(q,v)
expansion in Table
24
. The leading
q
order of the NS-R sector should give the 5d
\mathfrak{so}(9)+\bf S
theory. To our knowledge, 5d
\mathfrak{so}(9)+\bf S
theory does not have an ADHM construction. Luckily, the exact
v
expansion formula of the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(9)+2\mathbf{V}+\bf S
theory has been found in
(
DelZotto:2018tcj
, Equation (H.29))
. By decoupling the two
\bf V
, we obtain the following exact
v
expansion formula

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(9)+\mathbf{S}}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(9)}_{n\theta}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(2)_{s}}v^{2n+6}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(0n01)}\chi^{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}_{(1)_{s}}v^{2n+7}+\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}_{(0n10)}v^{2n+8}.

(4.139)

This is in complete agreement with our leading
q
order of the NS-R elliptic genus whose coefficients are colored red in Table
24
. Utilizing the Weyl dimension formula, we further find the following rational expression of
v
with all gauge and flavor fugacities turned off:

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(9)+\mathbf{S}}(v)=\frac{v^{6}\left(3v^{8}-20v^{7}+58v^{6}-116v^{5}+134v^{4}-116v^{3}+58v^{2}-20v+3\right)}{(v-1)^{8}(v+1)^{12}}.

(4.140)

On the other hand, the leading
q
order of the R-R sector should give the 5d
\mathfrak{so}(9)+4\bf V+S
theory. The exact
v
expansion formula of the one-instanton partition function of 5d
\mathfrak{so}(9)+4\mathbf{V}+3\bf S
theory has been found in
(
Gu:2020fem
, Equation (E.6))
. By decoupling two
\bf S
, we find the resulting 5d partition function is in agreement with the leading
q
order of the one-string R-R elliptic genus:

\displaystyle Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{so}(9)+4\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{S}}(v)=-\frac{v^{2}\left(v^{8}+12v^{7}+66v^{6}+268v^{5}+954v^{4}+268v^{3}+66v^{2}+12v+1\right)}{(v+1)^{12}},

up to an overall sign. The Fourier coefficients are colored orange in Table
24
.

\mathfrak{su}(3)+12\bf F

\mathbb{E}_{1}

b_{ij}
in Table

. The symmetry coefficient matrix indicates the self-dual spectral flow symmetry as expected. The 5d one-instanton partition function of
\mathfrak{su}(2)+6\bf F

Z_{1}^{\mathfrak{su}(2)+6\bf F}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Big(\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(12)}_{S}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{n\theta}v^{1+2n}-\chi^{\mathfrak{so}(12)}_{C}\chi^{\mathfrak{su}(2)}_{f+n\theta}v^{2+2n}\Big).

(4.141)

where
\mathfrak{so}(12)
is the pertubative 5d flavor symmetry and

and

are the spinor and conjugate spinor representations which both have dimension 32.

.

5
Modular bootstrap of the twisted elliptic genera

In this section, we utilize the modularity of elliptic genera to obtain some all order results beyond the
q
expansion. This compensates the results of twisted elliptic genera for
\mathfrak{n}=1,2
twisted theories where from elliptic blowup equations we can only solve
\mathbb{E}_{1}
as
q
expansion but cannot have a compact formula. The modular bootstrap approach views the elliptic genera as Jacobi forms on certain modular groups, and
utilizes the finite generation property of modular forms and Jacobi forms to obtain a compact modular expression of elliptic genera which works for all
q
orders.

5.1
The modular ansatz for twisted elliptic genera

For a 6d
(1,0)
SCFT with gauge group
G
and tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
, the modular ansatz for its reduced one-string elliptic genus was proposed by Del Zotto and Lockhart in
DelZotto:2018tcj
as

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta^{12(\mathfrak{n}-2)-4+24\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{h^{\vee}_{G}-1}}.

(5.143)

Here all gauge and flavor fugacities are turned off, and only
\epsilon_{+}
serves as the elliptic parameter of the Jacobi form.
The
\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}
comes from the special phenomenon of
\mathfrak{n}=1
theories, where the leading
q
order of elliptic genus is always trivial, and the 5d theory information only emerges from the subleading
q
order. Utilizing several other constraints, mostly from the universal features of the leading order of R-R and NS-R elliptic genera,
DelZotto:2018tcj
successfully determined the modular ansatz for most rank-one theories. The modular ansatz for several remaining theories was determined in
Gu:2020fem
with the help of elliptic blowup equations.

We would like to find a generalization of (
5.143
) for the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genera.
An inspiration comes from the recent study on the modularity of topological strings on
N
-section Calabi-Yau threefolds
Cota:2019cjx
;
Knapp:2021vkm
;
Schimannek:2021pau
. The modular ansatz for the topological string partition function on such geometries was first proposed in
Cota:2019cjx
. Combining (
5.143
) and the results in
Cota:2019cjx
together, we propose the following modular ansatz for the reduced one-string elliptic genus for twisted 6d (1,0) theories with twist coefficient
N=2,3,4
:

\boxed{\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta^{12(\mathfrak{n}-2)-4+24\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}}\Delta_{2N}(\frac{\tau}{N})^{s}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}-1}},}

(5.144)

with

\boxed{s=\frac{N}{N-1}\Big(c-\frac{\mathfrak{n}-2}{2}-\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}\Big).}

(5.145)

Recall the
c
is the
c
constant of twisted theories defined in (
2.24
) that depends only on the tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
and twist coefficient
N
. The
\Delta_{2N}
are some
\Gamma_{0}(N)
cusp forms defined in Appendix
B
.
The numerator
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
6(\mathfrak{n}-2)+2Ns+12\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}-2h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}
and index
4({h^{\vee}_{\mathring{G}}-1})+\mathfrak{n}-h_{G}^{\vee}
. The
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
usually has fractional
q^{1/N}
orders thus it is convenient to scale
q^{1/N}
to
q
. Then we expect
\mathcal{N}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+})
to be a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
weak Jacobi form with positive integer index. By the finite generation property of weak Jacobi forms
eichler1985theory
:, we have

\mathcal{N}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+})\in M_{\star}(N)[\phi_{-2,1}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+})].

(5.146)

Here
M_{\star}(N)
denotes the ring of modular forms on
\Gamma_{1}(N)
. The
\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,z)
and
\phi_{0,1}(\tau,z)
are the well-known Eichler-Zagier generators for weak Jacobi forms whose expressions are collected in Appendix
B
. The
s
here measure the difference between the twisted
c
constant and the untwisted one and is related to a geometric quantity
r_{\beta}=Ns
which is always integral
Cota:2019cjx
. Interestingly, we find that except for the
\mathfrak{n}=1,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
twisted theory, the modular ansatz of all twisted one-string elliptic genera encountered in the current paper satisfy a bigger congruence subgroup that is
\Gamma_{0}(N)
. We collect the definition and modular generators for
\Gamma_{0}(N)
and
\Gamma_{1}(N)
in Appendix
B
.

Some more explanation for the
N=4
cases are needed. We notice that for some theories with twist coefficients
4
, it is possible that besides the
\Gamma_{1}(4)
elements from
\Delta_{8}
, there can be extra
\Gamma_{1}(2)
elements. In such case, the
\Delta_{8}(\frac{\tau}{4})^{s}
in (
5.144
) need to be extended to
\Delta_{8}(\frac{\tau}{4})^{s_{1}}\Delta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{4})^{s_{2}}
. This suggests that the associated Calabi-Yau geometries contain both 4-section and 2-section components.

We collect in Table
29
the relevant data for the modular ansatz for all rank-one twisted theories. The
\#
means the number of independent parameters to be fixed in the numerator
\mathcal{N}
. The
\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{so}(12)^{(2)}
theory is marked with
*
because we do not have enough data to fix the modular ansatz, as it involves half-hyper. Except for this theory, we successfully fix its modular ansatz for all other theories in Table
29
and check the ansatz against the results from twisted elliptic blowup equations to high
q
orders.
