Table 29:
Data of
\Gamma_{1}(N)
modular ansatz for the twisted one-string elliptic genera of twisted 6d
(1,0)
rank-one theories. For
N=4
, the
(s_{1},s_{2})
means that the denominator contains
\Delta_{8}(\frac{\tau}{4})^{s_{1}}\Delta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{4})^{s_{2}}
and only one
s
means the
(s,0)
.

5.2
Computational results

We pick some interesting theories to explicitly show the modular ansatz results. The results for other theories in Table
29
can be shared to interested readers upon request.

\mathfrak{n}=3,\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

For 6d pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory studied in Section
4.2.1
, let us first discuss the
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{0}
case. From the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{8}(\tau/4)^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{8}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}.

(5.147)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight 0 and index 4. Interestingly, we notice that in this case the
\Delta_{8}(\tau/4)^{\frac{1}{4}}
has taken care of all the
\Gamma_{0}(4)
elements such that
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
contains no
q^{1/4+n/2}
orders, i.e.,
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is a
\Gamma_{0}(2)
Jacobi form. Using the generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{4}(2\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
given in Appendix
B
, there are 9 parameters to fix the ansatz of
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
.
We find the ansatz can be determined by the known Fourier coefficients of
q^{0,1,2}
of
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{10368}\Big(\big(-4(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+18E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-9E_{4}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{4}+2E_{2}^{(2)}\big(2(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-9E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{3}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle\phantom{=\frac{1}{10368}\Big(}-6\big((E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-3E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}\phi_{0}^{2}-2E_{2}^{(2)}\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}^{3}-\phi_{0}^{4}\Big).

(5.148)

We then check the modular ansatz results to
q^{8}
order. Here
\phi_{-2},\phi_{0}
are the short notation of
\phi_{-2,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. On the other hand, if we regard
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
as a
\Gamma_{0}(4)
Jacobi form, there will be 15 coefficients to fix
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
using
E_{2}^{(2)}
and
E_{2}^{(4)}
given in Appendix
B
as modular generators. For the
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
case, the modular ansatz takes the same form as (
5.147
) and the
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is still of weight 0 and index 4. Amusingly, we find in this case the
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is again a
\Gamma_{0}(2)
Jacobi form which we determine to be

\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})=-\frac{1}{10368}\Big(\big(2(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-3E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}+2E_{2}^{(2)}\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}-\phi_{0}^{2}\Big)^{2}.

(5.149)

This numerator is still fixed by the known Fourier coefficients of
q^{0,1,2}
and then checked to
q^{8}
order against the twisted elliptic blowup equations.

\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}

For 6d pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)}
theory, from the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{4}(\tau/2)^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{20}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{4}}.

(5.150)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight 0 and index 14. Based on the relation between twists and modularity, the
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
should be a
\Gamma_{0}(2)
Jacobi form. Again using the generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{4}(2\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
, we find there are 64 coefficients to fix
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. They can be fixed from the known Fourier coefficients up to
q^{7}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})=

\displaystyle\,-\frac{1}{160489808068608}\Big(\big(2(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-3E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}+2E_{2}^{(2)}\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}-\phi_{0}^{2}\Big)\Big((64(E_{2}^{(2)})^{12}

\displaystyle-672E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{10}+3024E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{8}-9072E_{4}^{3}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}+17982E_{4}^{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}

\displaystyle-15066E_{4}^{5}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+5103E_{4}^{6})\phi_{-2}^{12}+6E_{2}^{(2)}\big(64(E_{2}^{(2)})^{10}-800E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{8}

\displaystyle+3456E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}-5616E_{4}^{3}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+2268E_{4}^{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-1053E_{4}^{5}\big)\phi_{-2}^{11}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle-18\big(64(E_{2}^{(2)})^{10}-720E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{8}+2544E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}-2340E_{4}^{3}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}

\displaystyle-1836E_{4}^{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+513E_{4}^{5}\big)\phi_{-2}^{10}\phi_{0}^{2}+2E_{2}^{(2)}\big(1696(E_{2}^{(2)})^{8}-18000E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}

\displaystyle+64368E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}-77760E_{4}^{3}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+1701E_{4}^{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{9}\phi_{0}^{3}-9\big(80(E_{2}^{(2)})^{8}

\displaystyle-1648E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}+8316E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}-12276E_{4}^{3}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-945E_{4}^{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{8}\phi_{0}^{4}

\displaystyle+108E_{2}^{(2)}\big(8(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}-72E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+332E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-597E_{4}^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{7}\phi_{0}^{5}

\displaystyle+12\big(128(E_{2}^{(2)})^{6}-690E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+846E_{4}^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+675E_{4}^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{6}\phi_{0}^{6}

\displaystyle+36E_{2}^{(2)}\big(8(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+8E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-63E_{4}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{5}\phi_{0}^{7}+135\big(2(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}-6E_{4}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}

\displaystyle+7E_{4}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{4}\phi_{0}^{8}+2E_{2}^{(2)}\big(52(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-123E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{3}\phi_{0}^{9}+6\big(8(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}-19E_{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}\phi_{0}^{10}

\displaystyle+18E_{2}^{(2)}\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}^{11}+7\phi_{0}^{12}\Big).

(5.151)

We then check the modular ansatz results to
q^{16}
against the 2d localization formula and find perfect agreement.

\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

For 6d pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory, from the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{6}(\tau/3)^{\frac{2}{3}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{20}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{3}}.

(5.152)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight 0 and index 10. The
\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
should be a
\Gamma_{0}(3)
Jacobi form. Using the generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(3)},E_{4}(3\tau),E_{6}(3\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
given in Appendix
B
, we find there are 67 coefficients to fix
\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. We find they can be fixed from the data up to
q^{7}
up to the vanishing relation (
B.225
), which involve in total 44 independent parameters. We obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{10319560704}\Big(E_{2}^{(3)}\big(2268(E_{2}^{(3)})^{9}-14661E_{4}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{7}+20880E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{6}

\displaystyle-25560E_{4}E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{4}+22176E_{6}^{2}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}-7488E_{4}E_{6}^{2}E_{2}^{(3)}+2560E_{6}^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{10}

\displaystyle+\!12\big(39(E_{2}^{(3)})^{9}-252E_{4}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{7}+300E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{6}-54E_{4}E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{4}\!-96E_{6}^{2}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}

\displaystyle+96E_{4}E_{6}^{2}E_{2}^{(3)}-128E_{6}^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{9}\phi_{0}+27\big(39(E_{2}^{(3)})^{8}-252E_{4}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{6}+344E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{5}

\displaystyle-336E_{4}E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}+256E_{6}^{2}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{2}+64E_{4}E_{6}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{8}\phi_{0}^{2}+24E_{2}^{(3)}\big(18(E_{2}^{(3)})^{6}

\displaystyle-117E_{4}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{4}+93E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}+234E_{4}E_{6}E_{2}^{(3)}-416E_{6}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{7}\phi_{0}^{3}-126\big(6(E_{2}^{(3)})^{6}

\displaystyle-39E_{4}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{4}+48E_{6}(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}-28E_{4}E_{6}E_{2}^{(3)}-16E_{6}^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{6}\phi_{0}^{4}-1512E_{4}E_{6}\phi_{-2}^{5}\phi_{0}^{5}

\displaystyle+42E_{2}^{(3)}\big(3(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}-18E_{4}E_{2}^{(3)}+20E_{6}\big)\phi_{-2}^{4}\phi_{0}^{6}+72(E_{2}^{(3)}E_{4}-E_{6})\phi_{-2}^{3}\phi_{0}^{7}

\displaystyle+27E_{4}\phi_{-2}^{2}\phi_{0}^{8}-4E_{2}^{(3)}\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}^{9}-3\phi_{0}^{10}\Big).

(5.153)

Here
\phi_{-2},\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
.
We then check the modular ansatz results to
q^{16}
against the twisted elliptic genera solved from the recursion formula.

\mathfrak{n}=6,E_{6}^{(2)}

For 6d pure
E_{6}^{(2)}
theory, the general formula (
5.144
) gives the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{4}(\tau/2)^{\frac{3}{2}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{44}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{8}}.

(5.154)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight 0 and index 26. The
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
should be a
\Gamma_{0}(2)
Jacobi form. Using the generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{4}(2\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
, we find there are 196 coefficients to fix
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. Indeed we find they can be fixed from the data up to
q^{13}
. We then check the modular ansatz results against the twisted elliptic genera solved from the recursion formula to
q^{14}
. The explicit formula for
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is too long to show here.

E-string

The elliptic genera of E-strings have been studied in
Haghighat:2014pva
;
Kim:2014dza
;
Cai:2014vka
;
Kim:2015fxa
;
Duan:2018sqe
. For a 6d theory with trivial gauge group in the tensor branch, there is no discrete symmetry from the automorphism of the gauge algebra. However, one can still consider discrete symmetries of the flavor algebra. A typical example is the E-string theory, where the flavor symmetry is the exceptional group
E_{8}
, for which we may find a rich class of discrete subgroups. The twisted circle compactification with such a discrete symmetry shall lead to a theory whose partition function is the same as the partition function of E-strings on a circle with a special choice of the Wilson line parameters. At first glance, it looks like these theories are boring, but as have been studied in
Cota:2019cjx
;
Knapp:2021vkm
, their geometries, which are genus-one fibrations with
N
-sections, have very rich structures. Thus it is worthy to have a study on them. For example, let us do a
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist to E-string theory formally. The twisted one-string elliptic genus is just

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{i})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{(r,s)=(1,4),(4,1)}^{(2,3),(3,2)}\prod_{i=1}^{4}\prod_{j=5}^{8}\frac{\theta_{r}(m_{i})\theta_{s}(m_{j})}{\eta^{2}}.

(5.155)

Turning off the flavor parameters, the reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus is independent from
\epsilon_{+}
and has the following nice modular ansatz as a special case of our general formula (
5.144
):

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\theta_{2}^{4}\theta_{3}^{4}}{\eta^{8}}=\frac{16\Delta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2})}{\eta^{8}}.

(5.156)

It is easy to prove this identity. This has been also studied in the setting of Calabi-Yau threefolds with 2-sections in
Cota:2019cjx
.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(2)^{(2)}

This case is similar to the E-string theory, where the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist is only performed on the flavor parameters.
From the general formula (
5.144
), we find the following modular ansatz

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{-4}\Delta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}.

(5.157)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
-2
and index 4. In the case, the
\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is a
\Gamma_{0}(2)
Jacobi form.
We fix it to be

\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\phi_{-2}}{864}\big(E^{(2)}_{2}\phi_{-2}-\phi_{0}\big)\left(2(E^{(2)}_{2})^{2}\phi_{-2}^{2}-3E_{4}\phi_{-2}^{2}+2E^{(2)}_{2}\phi_{0}\phi_{-2}-\phi_{0}^{2}\right).

(5.158)

Recall
\phi_{-2}
and
\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})
.
We have checked this modular ansatz against the localization formula (
4.121
) to
\mathcal{O}(q^{20})
. In fact, we find an even simpler form for the numerator:

\mathcal{N}(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})=2\phi_{-2}(\epsilon_{+})h(\epsilon_{+})^{2}h(2\epsilon_{+}),\qquad h(\tau,z)=\frac{\theta_{4}(2\tau,z)}{\theta_{4}(2\tau)}.

(5.159)

Here we notice that

E^{(2)}_{2}\phi_{-2}-\phi_{0}=-12h(\epsilon_{+})^{2},\\

(5.160)

2(E^{(2)}_{2})^{2}\phi_{-2}^{2}-3E_{4}\phi_{-2}^{2}+2E^{(2)}_{2}\phi_{0}\phi_{-2}-\phi_{0}^{2}=-144h(2\epsilon_{+}).

(5.161)

In summary, we find the following compact formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genus:

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\theta_{2}(\tau)\theta_{3}(\tau)\eta(\tau)\theta_{4}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}{\theta_{2}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2}\theta_{3}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2}}=\frac{2\eta(\tau)^{5}}{\eta(\frac{\tau}{2})^{2}}\frac{\theta_{4}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}{\theta_{2}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2}\theta_{3}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2}}

(5.162)

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

From the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{-4}\Delta_{8}(\tau/4)^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}.

(5.163)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
-2
and index 3. We find that unlike the pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
case, now the
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
does contain quarter
q
orders. Therefore, there is no longer simplification to
\Gamma_{0}(2)
, i.e., the
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
should be a genuine
\Gamma_{0}(4)
Jacobi form. Using the
\Gamma_{0}(4)
modular generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
given in Appendix
B
, we find there are 6 parameters to fix the modular ansatz. By the known coefficients of
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
up to
q^{6}
, we determine it to be

\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{1}{288}\phi_{-2}\big((E_{2}^{(2)}+3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}-4\phi_{0}\big)\big((E_{2}^{(2)}-3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}+2\phi_{0}\big).

(5.164)

Here
\phi_{-2}
and
\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. We then check the modular ansatz against the results from blowup equations to
q^{16}
. In fact, we observe an even simpler form for the numerator:

\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})=-4\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})h(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2}h(\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\qquad h(\tau,z)=\frac{\theta_{4}(2\tau,z)}{\theta_{4}(2\tau)}.

(5.165)

Here we notice that

(E_{2}^{(2)}-3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}+2\phi_{0}=24h(2\tau,\epsilon_{+})^{2},\\

(5.166)

(E_{2}^{(2)}+3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}-4\phi_{0}=-48h(\tau,\epsilon_{+}).

(5.167)

In summary, we find the following compact formula for the twisted one-string elliptic genus:

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{4\eta(\frac{\tau}{4})^{2}\theta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2},\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\frac{\tau}{2})^{2}\theta_{4}(\frac{\tau}{2})}\Big(\frac{\theta_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})\theta_{4}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\theta_{1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})\theta_{4}(\tau)}\Big)^{2}.

(5.168)

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(5)^{(2)}

From the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{-4}\Delta_{8}(\tau/4)^{\frac{1}{4}}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{2}}.

(5.169)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
-4
and index 5. We find the
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
should be a
\Gamma_{0}(4)
Jacobi form. Using the
\Gamma_{0}(4)
modular generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
, we find there are 10 parameters to fix the modular ansatz. By the known coefficients of
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
up to
q^{4}
, we determine it to be

\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})=-\frac{1}{1728}\phi_{-2}^{2}\big((E_{2}^{(2)}+3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}-4\phi_{0}\big)\big((E_{2}^{(2)}-3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}+2\phi_{0}\big)^{2}.

(5.170)

Here
\phi_{-2}
and
\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. We then check the modular ansatz against the results from blowup equations to
q^{12}
.

\mathfrak{n}=1,\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

The twisted one-string elliptic genus has been solved from blowup equations in early sections and the Fourier coefficients have been shown in Table
26
.
From the general formula (
5.144
), we have the following modular ansatz

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{8}^{\frac{5}{4}}({\tau}/{4})\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta^{8}\Delta_{4}^{2}({\tau}/{4})\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})}.

(5.171)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
0
and index 2. Note there are both
\Delta_{8}
and
\Delta_{4}
which indicates that both
\Gamma_{0}(4)
and
\Gamma_{0}(2)
play a role. The
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is a genuine
\Gamma_{0}(4)
Jacobi form. Using the
\Gamma_{0}(4)
modular generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
given in Appendix
B
, we find there are 6 parameters to fix for the modular ansatz of
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. Indeed, they are fixed by the known
q^{0,1,2}
coefficients. We have

\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{4}{3}E_{2}^{(4)}\phi_{-2}\big((E_{2}^{(2)}-3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}+2\phi_{0}\big).

(5.172)

Here
\phi_{-2}
and
\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
.
We then check the modular ansatz against the twisted elliptic genera solved from blowup equations to
q^{16}
order.

\mathfrak{n}=1,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

In the end we show one example of
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist which is the only case we encounter in the current work that the modular group must be
\Gamma_{1}(N)
instead of
\Gamma_{0}(N)
. This is the
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=1
. The general formula (
5.144
) gives the following modular ansatz for its reduced twisted one-string elliptic genus

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})=\frac{\Delta_{6}(\tau/3)^{\frac{1}{6}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})}{\eta(\tau)^{8}\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,2\epsilon_{+})^{3}}.

(5.173)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+})
is of weight
-3
and index 7. Due to the odd weight, the
\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
must be a
\Gamma_{1}(3)
Jacobi form instead of a
\Gamma_{0}(3)
one. Using the generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(3)}
,
E_{4}(3\tau)
,
E_{3}^{(a)}(\tau)
,
E_{3}^{(b)}(\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
given in Appendix
B
, we find there are 26 coefficients to fix
\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})
. Indeed they can be fixed from the data up to
q^{3}
up to the vanishing relations (
B.228
). We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(3\tau,\epsilon_{+})=

\displaystyle-\frac{\phi_{-2}^{3}}{186624}\Big(E_{2}^{(3)}\big(19(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}E_{3}^{(a)}\!+450(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}E_{3}^{(b)}\!-45E_{4}E_{2}^{(3)}E_{3}^{(a)}\!-64(E_{3}^{(a)})^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{4}

\displaystyle\ +6\big(17(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}E_{3}^{(a)}-342(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}E_{3}^{(b)}+45E_{4}E_{2}^{(3)}E_{3}^{(a)}+16(E_{3}^{(a)})^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{3}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle\ -27\big(21(E_{2}^{(3)})^{2}E_{3}^{(a)}-72(E_{2}^{(3)})^{2}E_{3}^{(b)}+11E_{4}E_{3}^{(a)}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}\phi_{0}^{2}+36E_{2}^{(3)}(19E_{3}^{(a)}

\displaystyle\ +69E_{3}^{(b)})\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}^{3}-18(11E_{3}^{(a)}+189E_{3}^{(b)})\phi_{0}^{4}\Big).

(5.174)

We then checked it to
q^{11}
order against the twisted elliptic blowup equations and found perfect agreement.

5.3
Full modular ansatz with gauge fugacities

It is also interesting to consider the full modular ansatz with gauge fugacities. For untwisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs, this has been studied in
DelZotto:2017mee
for pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
and
\mathfrak{so}(8)
theories, see also
Kim:2018gak
;
Duan:2020imo
. The denominator of the modular ansatz of reduced one-string elliptic genus involves the block

\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}^{+}(G)}\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}\pm 2\epsilon_{+}).

(5.175)

This is natural as all the poles of elliptic genera are associated to the 5d states related to the long roots of
G
.
Analogously for twisted theories, given the understanding on 5d circle reduction theory, it is reasonable to expect the the denominator of the modular ansatz for twisted one-string elliptic genera involves the block

\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}^{+}(\mathring{G})}\theta_{1}(m_{\alpha}\pm 2\epsilon_{+}),

(5.176)

where
\Delta_{l}^{+}
is the long positive roots of
\mathring{G}
. More precisely, we propose the following full modular ansatz for the reduced one-string twisted elliptic genera:

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})=\frac{\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})}{\eta^{12(\mathfrak{n}-2)-4+24\delta_{\mathfrak{n},1}}\Delta_{2N}(\frac{\tau}{N})^{s}\prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_{l}^{+}(\mathring{G})}\phi_{-1,1/2}(m_{\alpha}\pm 2\epsilon_{+})},

(5.177)

where the
s
is defined the same as in (
5.145
). The
\phi_{-1,1/2}(\tau,z):=\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,z)^{1/2}
is a Jacobi form with weight
-1
and index
1/2
. The weight and index of
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})
should be coordinated with the weight 0 and index (
2.44
) of the
\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})
. In general, for twist coefficient
N
, the
\mathcal{N}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+},m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}})
should be a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
Jacobi form of multi elliptic variables. In principle, one can fix the numerator by the
M_{*}(N)
modular generators, the
\phi_{-2,1}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(N\tau,\epsilon_{+})
generators, the generators of
\mathring{G}
Weyl invariant weak Jacobi forms and the generators of
\mathring{F}
Weyl invariant weak Jacobi forms all together.

We give a simple example here, which is the pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=3
. The low energy algebra is
\mathring{G}=\mathfrak{sp}(1)
. We have the following anstaz for the reduced one-string elliptic genus with
\mathfrak{sp}(1)
fugacity
m
as

\mathbb{E}_{1}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)=\frac{\Delta_{8}(\tau/4)^{\frac{1}{4}}\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)}{\eta(\tau)^{2}\theta_{1}(2m\pm 2\epsilon_{+})}.

(5.178)

Here
\mathcal{N}(\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)
has weight 0 and index 4 for
\epsilon_{+}
and index 1 for
m
. We find the
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)
is a
\Gamma_{0}(4)
Jacobi form with two elliptic variables. Using the
\Gamma_{0}(4)
modular generators
E_{2}(\tau)^{(2)},E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau)
and
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+}),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
and
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,m),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,m)
given in Appendix
B
, we find there are 35 parameters to fix for the modular ansatz of
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)
. Indeed, they are fixed by the known coefficients up to
q^{5}
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{127401984}\Big((5E_{2}^{(2)}-9E_{2}^{(4)})\varphi_{-2}+4\varphi_{0}\Big)\Big(\big((E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}+636E_{2}^{(4)}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{3}

\displaystyle-3834(E_{2}^{(4)})^{2}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+8316(E_{2}^{(4)})^{3}E_{2}^{(2)}-6399(E_{2}^{(4)})^{4}\big)\phi_{-2}^{4}-16\big((E_{2}^{(2)})^{3}

\displaystyle-117E_{2}^{(4)}(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}+567(E_{2}^{(4)})^{2}E_{2}^{(2)}-675(E_{2}^{(4)})^{3}\big)\phi_{-2}^{3}\phi_{0}+96\big((E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}

\displaystyle+30E_{2}^{(4)}E_{2}^{(2)}-63(E_{2}^{(4)})^{2}\big)\phi_{-2}^{2}\phi_{0}^{2}-256(E_{2}^{(2)}-3E_{2}^{(4)})\phi_{-2}\phi_{0}^{3}+256\phi_{0}^{4}\Big).

Here
\varphi_{-2},\varphi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,m),\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,m)
, while
\phi_{-2},\phi_{0}
are short for
\phi_{-2,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
and
\phi_{0,1}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+})
.
We then check the modular ansatz against the twisted elliptic genera from 2d localization formula to
q^{10}
order. Note in
\mathcal{N}(4\tau,\epsilon_{+},m)
the
m
and
\epsilon_{+}
dependence naturally factorizes. This is expected as there is no
m\epsilon_{+}
term in its index quadratic form.

6
Twisting from Higgsing

Many twisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs can be Higgsed from untwisted 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs
Kim:2019dqn
;
Hayashi:2021pcj
. Most previously known examples are recognized from brane webs. In particular, many examples of twisting from Higgsing of
\mathfrak{so}
type with
\mathfrak{n}=2,3,4
were discussed in
Kim:2019dqn
. In this section, we systematically study all possible twisting from Higgsing among rank-one theories and propose a simple method to obtain the precise Higgsing condition that does not rely on brane webs, but only on Lie algebra representations and their decompositions.

A primary condition to establish a Higgsing is that the two theories must have the same tensor coefficients
\mathfrak{n}
.
Suppose we want to establish a Higgsing from an untwisted theory with gauge group
\mathcal{G}
to a twisted theory
G^{(n)}
with low energy gauge group
\mathring{G}
. Our trick is to perform the decomposition

\mathcal{G}\to\mathring{G}\times H_{res}.

(6.179)

Here the residue group
H_{res}
is either
\mathfrak{su}(2)
or
\mathfrak{so}(2)
for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist, or
\mathfrak{su}(3)
for
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twist. The Higgsing to twisted theory can be achieved by discretizing
H_{res}
, i.e., to take its continuous fugacities as some fractions of
\tau
. In the meantime, some mass parameters of the hypermulitplets of the untwisted theory must be taken as some delicate linear combinations of
\epsilon_{+}
and
\tau
to produce the correct one-loop partition function of the twisted theory. We find all previously known examples of twisting from Higgsing fall into this paradigm. For example, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted pure
\mathfrak{su}(3)
can be obtained from Higgsing of
G_{2}+\bf F
theory. Here we show this from a purely algebraic viewpoint. Consider the decomposition
G_{2}\to\mathfrak{su}(2)\times\mathfrak{su}(2)
, we have the following representation decompositions

\displaystyle\mathbf{14}\rightarrow(\mathbf{2},\mathbf{4})+(\mathbf{3},\mathbf{1})+(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{3}),\qquad\mathbf{7}\rightarrow(\mathbf{1},\mathbf{3})+(\mathbf{2},\mathbf{2}).

(6.180)

The Higgsing process can be realized by simply discretizing the second
\mathfrak{su}(2)
, which we have presented in (
2.34
). To check the one-loop part, it is only necessary to study the function
f(\epsilon_{+},m_{G},m_{F},q)
inside the vector multiplet partition function

\mathrm{PE}\left(\frac{f(\epsilon_{+},m_{G},m_{F},q)}{\big(q_{1}^{1/2}-q_{1}^{-1/2}\big)\big(q_{2}^{1/2}-q_{2}^{-1/2}\big)(1-q)}\right).

(6.181)

Under analytic continuations, we have

\displaystyle\left(-v\chi_{\bf 14}^{G_{2}}+e^{m}\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}\right)\Big|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 3}^{A_{1}}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{A_{1}}(q^{3/4}+q^{1/4}+q^{-1/4}+q^{-3/4})\right)+vq^{-1/2}\left(\chi_{\bf 2}^{A_{1}}(q^{1/4}+q^{-1/4})\right),

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 3}^{A_{1}}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{A_{1}}q^{1/4}+\chi_{\bf 2}^{A_{1}}q^{3/4}\right).

Here we have dropped those gauge singlets by
\sim
. This computation shows that the Higgsing indeed gives the required one-loop part of the twisted
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}
theory.

A known infinite series of twisting from Higgsing is from
\mathfrak{so}(2k+9)+(2k+1)\mathbf{V}
to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of
\mathfrak{so}(2k+8)+2k\mathbf{V}
with
k=0,1,2,\dots
. This type of Higgsing has been discussed from the viewpoint of brane webs in Section 5 of
Kim:2019dqn
. Since the low energy gauge algebra of
\mathfrak{so}(2k+8)^{(2)}
is
\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)
, we can consider the decomposition
\mathfrak{so}(2k+9)\to\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)\times\mathfrak{so}(2)
. Let us just regard the
\mathfrak{so}(2)
as
\mathfrak{u}(1)
. It is useful to remark the following representation decompositions

\bf Adj\to(Adj+1)_{0}+V_{\pm 2},\hskip 22.99988ptV\to V_{0}+1_{\pm 2}.

(6.182)

To be precise, we find the Higgsing condition is

m_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\to\frac{\tau}{4},\qquad m_{j}\to m_{j},\ m_{j+k}\to m_{j}+\frac{\tau}{2},\ j=1,2,\dots,k,\quad m_{{\mathbf{V}}_{2k+1}}\to\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{2}.

(6.183)

Here we have used the orthogonal bases of
\mathfrak{so}(2n+1)
algebras such that the character of vector represention is
\chi_{\bf V}=1+\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q_{i}^{\pm 1}
. During the decomposition
m_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}=2\log(Q_{k+4})
.
For the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\bigg(-v\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+9)}+\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+9)}\sum_{i=1}^{2k+1}e^{m_{i}}\bigg)\Big|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}+\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}(q^{-1/2}+q^{1/2})\right)+vq^{-1/2}\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}+\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{k}e^{m_{i}},

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}+\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf V}^{\mathfrak{so}(2k+7)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{k}e^{m_{i}}.

(6.184)

This shows that the above Higgsing indeed produces the correct one-loop part of the
\mathfrak{so}(2k+8)^{(2)}
twisted theory.
It is also easy to check the elliptic genera obtained from the above Higgsing is precisely the same with expression from 2d localization.

The Higgsing trees for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted
\mathfrak{so}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=2,3,4
have been mostly studied in
Kim:2019dqn
using brane webs. We find more examples by our algebraic procedure, and collect the
\mathfrak{n}=4
Higgsing tree in Figure
2
,
\mathfrak{n}=3
Higgsing tree in Figure
3
and
\mathfrak{n}=2
Higgsing tree in Figure
4
. For
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twisted
\mathfrak{so}
theories with
\mathfrak{n}=1
, it is not easy to find the brane webs. We use our new method to propose the
\mathfrak{n}=1
Higgsing tree in Figure
5
.

\,\mathfrak{so}(11)+3\mathbf{V}\!\!

Higgsing tree for untwisted/twisted 6d

\mathfrak{n}=1
.

Our method can also be used to rule out some potential Higgsing. For example, we notice that the
\mathfrak{n}=2
,
\mathfrak{su}(N)
theory cannot be Higgsed to
\mathfrak{n}=2
,
\mathfrak{su}(N-1)^{(2)}
twisted theory. The reason is that the
\bf Adj
and
\bf\Lambda^{2}
of
\mathfrak{su}(N)
always have the same KK charge
0
under representation decompositions, thus can not produce the required one-loop function of the
\mathfrak{su}(N-1)^{(2)}
twisted theory.

The Higgsing of elliptic blowup equations for 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs have been discussed in Section 3.3 of
Gu:2020fem
. For the twisted cases, the Higgsing is just similar. The Higgsing conditions can be taken directly in the elliptic genera as well as the (twisted) elliptic blowup equations, at least for the unity ones. It is easy to check that the characteristic
a
and value
y
of elliptic blowup equations are consistent with the Higgsing.

In the following, we pick some interesting examples of twisting from Higgsing to show our algebraic approach and give the precise Higgsing conditions.

6.1
Higgsing from untwisted to twisted theories

6.1.1
E_{7}\to E_{6}^{(2)}

A series of interesting examples of twisting from Higgising is the
E_{7}\to E_{6}^{(2)}
type for
\mathfrak{n}=6,5,4,3,2,1
. Let us start with the simplest case
\mathfrak{n}=6
, i.e., Higgsing
E_{7}+\mathbf{F}\rightarrow E_{6}^{(2)}.

The flavor algebra of
E_{7}+\mathbf{F}
theory is
\mathfrak{so}(2)_{12}
.
To write down the precise Higgsing condition, we first decompose
E_{7}\to F_{4}\times\mathfrak{su}(2)
. It is useful to remark the following representation decompositions

\mathbf{133}\to\bf(1,3)+(26,3)+(52,1),\hskip 22.99988pt56\to(1,4)+(26,2).

(6.185)

We find the Higgsing can be taken by discretizing the
\mathfrak{su}(2)
into
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
:

m_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\to\frac{\tau}{2},\quad m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}\to\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{4}.

(6.186)

It is easy to see that upon this condition the elliptic genera index of
E_{7}+\mathbf{F}
from (
2.43
)

\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{E_{7}},m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)})=-14d\epsilon_{+}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}(3d^{2}-2d)+d\left(-3(m,m)_{E_{7}}+6m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}^{2}\right)

(6.187)

indeed goes to the index of twisted elliptic genera of
E_{6}^{(2)}
theory from (
2.44
)

\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{F_{4}})=-8d\epsilon_{+}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}(3d^{2}-2d)+d(-3(m,m)_{F_{4}}).

(6.188)

To check the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\left(-v\chi_{\bf 133}^{E_{7}}+e^{m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}}\chi_{\bf 56}^{E_{7}}\right)\Big|_{Higgsing}

(6.189)

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 52}^{F_{4}}+\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}(q^{1/2}+1+q^{-1/2})\right)+vq^{-1/4}\left(\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}(q^{1/4}+q^{-1/4})\right),

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 52}^{F_{4}}+\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}q^{1/2}\right).

Here we have droped those gauge singlets.

For
\mathfrak{n}=4
, we can Higgs
E_{7}+2\mathbf{F}\rightarrow(E_{6}+2\mathbf{F})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

The Higgsing condition is precisely (
6.186
) combined with
m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}=m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}+\tau/4
. For the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\left(-v\chi_{\bf 133}^{E_{7}}+e^{m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}}\chi_{\bf 56}^{E_{7}}+e^{m^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}}\chi_{\bf 56}^{E_{7}}\right)\Big|_{Higgsing}

(6.190)

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 52}^{F_{4}}+\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}(q^{1/2}+1+q^{-1/2})\right)+(vq^{-1/4}+e^{m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}}q^{1/4})\left(\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}(q^{1/4}+q^{-1/4})\right),

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 52}^{F_{4}}+\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}q^{1/2}\right)+e^{m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}}\left(\chi_{\bf 26}^{F_{4}}(1+q^{1/2})\right).

The same applies to all Higgsing

E_{7}+\frac{8-\mathfrak{n}}{2}\mathbf{F}\rightarrow(E_{6}+(6-\mathfrak{n})\mathbf{F})/\mathbb{Z}_{2},\qquad\mathfrak{n}=6,4,2.

(6.191)

For odd
\mathfrak{n}
, the Higgsing is a bit unorthodox as the twisted theory has unpaired matter content. Under the above procedure, the
\frac{1}{2}\bf 56
with zero mass should produce a
\bf 26
with a quarter KK-charge and zero mass.

As a final remark, some 6d
E_{6}^{(2)}
theories can also be Higgsed from conformal matter theories. For example, it was found in
Hayashi:2021pcj
that the
\mathfrak{n}=2,E_{6}^{(2)}
theory can be obtained from a special Higgsing of the minimal
(E_{7},E_{7})
conformal matter theory, while the
\mathfrak{n}=5,E_{6}^{(2)}
theory can be obtained from a special Higgsing of extended
(E_{7},\underline{E_{7}})
theory.

6.1.2
E_{6}\to\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}

One more interesting example of twisting from Higgsing is for the
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
twisted theories. In
Hayashi:2021pcj
, it was found that the
\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory can be obtained from a special Higgsing of the minimal
(E_{6},E_{6})
conformal matter theory, while the
\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory can be obtained from a special Higgsing of non-minimal
(E_{6},E_{6})_{2}
conformal matter theory. We notice that in general, the rank-one
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory can be Higgsed from the rank-one
E_{6}
theory with the same tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
. Let us first consider the
\mathfrak{n}=4
case, i.e. Higgsing
E_{6}+2\mathbf{F}
to pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
. To write down the precise Higgsing condition, it is useful to consider decomposition
E_{6}\to\mathfrak{su}(3)\times G_{2}
. Remark that

\mathbf{78}\to\bf(8,1)+(8,7)+(1,14),\hskip 22.99988pt27\to(\bar{6},1)+(3,7).

(6.192)

We then observe that the Higgsing to twisting can be achieved by discretizing the
\mathfrak{su}(3)
into
\mathbb{Z}_{3}
by the following condition

m_{1,2,3}^{\mathfrak{su}(3)}\to 0,\pm\frac{\tau}{3},\qquad m_{1,2}\to\epsilon_{+},\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{3}.

(6.193)

It is easy to see that upon this condition the elliptic genera index of
E_{6}+2\mathbf{F}
from (
2.43
)

\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{E_{6}},m_{1,2})=-14d\epsilon_{+}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}(3d^{2}-2d)+d\left(-3(m,m)_{E_{6}}+6m_{\mathfrak{so}(2)}^{2}\right)

(6.194)

indeed goes to the index of twisted elliptic genera of
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory from (
2.44
)

\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{G_{2}})=-8d\epsilon_{+}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}(3d^{2}-2d)+d(-3(m,m)_{G_{2}}).

(6.195)

For the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\left(-v\chi_{\bf 78}^{E_{6}}+(e^{m_{1}}+e^{m_{2}})\chi_{\bf 27}^{E_{6}}\right)\Big|_{Higgsing}

(6.196)

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 14}^{G_{2}}+\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(2+2q^{\pm 1/3}+q^{\pm 2/3})\right)+(vq^{-1/3}+v)\left(\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(q^{1/3}+1+q^{-1/3})\right),

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 14}^{G_{2}}+\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(q^{1/3}+q^{2/3})\right).

This shows that the Higgsing indeed produces the correct one-loop function of pure
\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
theory.

For
\mathfrak{n}=3
, we can Higgs
E_{6}+3\mathbf{F}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{so}(8)+\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{C})/\mathbb{Z}_{3}.

The Higgsing condition is precisely (
6.193
) combined with
m_{3}=m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}+\tau/3
. For the one-loop part, we have

\displaystyle\left(-v\chi_{\bf 78}^{E_{6}}+(e^{m_{1}}+e^{m_{2}}+e^{m_{3}})\chi_{\bf 27}^{E_{6}}\right)\Big|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 14}^{G_{2}}+\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(2+2q^{\pm 1/3}+q^{\pm 2/3})\right)+(vq^{-1/3}+v+e^{m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}}q^{1/3})(q^{1/3}+1+q^{-1/3})\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}},

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 14}^{G_{2}}+\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(q^{1/3}+q^{2/3})\right)+e^{m_{\mathfrak{sp}(1)}}\chi_{\bf 7}^{G_{2}}(1+q^{1/3}+q^{2/3}).

The same applies to all Higgsing to twisting

E_{6}+(6-\mathfrak{n})\mathbf{F}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{so}(8)+(4-\mathfrak{n})(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{S}+\mathbf{C}))/\mathbb{Z}_{3},\qquad\mathfrak{n}=4,3,2,1.

(6.197)

6.1.3
\mathfrak{so}(7)\to\mathfrak{su}(4)^{(2)}

Consider the
\mathfrak{n}=2
Higgsing from
\mathfrak{so}(7)+\bf V+4S
to the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of
\mathfrak{su}(4)+8\bf F
. This twisting from Higgsing has been found in
Kim:2019dqn
from brane webs. Here we adopt a purely algebraic approach. Let us consider the decomposition
\mathfrak{so}(7)\to\mathfrak{sp}(2)\times\mathfrak{so}(2)
. Regarding the
\mathfrak{so}(2)
as
\mathfrak{u}(1)
. We have the following representation decompositions

\mathbf{21}\to\bf 1_{0}+5_{2}+5_{-2}+10_{0},\hskip 22.99988pt7\to 1_{2}+1_{-2}+5_{0},\hskip 22.99988pt8\to 4_{1}+4_{-1}.

(6.198)

We find the Higgsing to twisting can be achieved by discretizing the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
by the following condition

m_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\to\frac{\tau}{4},\quad m_{\bf 7}\to\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{2},\quad m_{i,\bf 8}\to m_{i,\bf 4}+\frac{\tau}{4},\ i=1,2,3,4.

(6.199)

For the one-loop part, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(-v\chi_{\bf 21}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}+e^{m_{\bf 7}}\chi_{\bf 7}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}+\chi_{\bf 8}^{\mathfrak{so}(7)}\sum_{i=1}^{4}e^{m_{i,\bf 8}}\bigg)\bigg|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf 10}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}+\chi_{\bf 5}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}q^{\pm 1/2}\right)+vq^{-1/2}\chi_{\bf 5}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}+(q^{-1/4}+q^{1/4})\chi_{\bf 4}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}\sum_{i=1}^{4}q^{1/4}e^{m_{i,\bf 4}},

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf 10}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}+\chi_{\bf 5}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}q^{1/2}\right)+\left(\chi_{\bf 4}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}+\chi_{\bf 4}^{\mathfrak{sp}(2)}q^{1/2}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{4}e^{m_{i,\bf 4}}.

Thus we successfully reach the one-loop function of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of
\mathfrak{n}=2
,
\mathfrak{su}(4)+8\bf F
theory.

6.2
Higgsing from twisted to twisted theories

Let us consider a chain of Higgsing
\mathfrak{n}=2
,
\mathfrak{su}(N)^{(2)}\to\mathfrak{su}(N-1)^{(2)}
. From (
2.44
), we can easily write down the modular indices of the
\mathfrak{su}(N)^{(2)}
theories. For both
N=2r+1
and
N=2r
cases, we have

\mathbb{E}_{d}(\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},m_{\mathfrak{sp}(r)},m_{\mathfrak{sp}(N)})=-Nd\epsilon_{+}^{2}+d^{2}\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}+d\bigg(-(m,m)_{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i,\bf N}^{2}\bigg).

(6.200)

Consider the Higgsing from
\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}\to\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}
. The low energy gauge group
\mathfrak{sp}(r)
remains the same upon the Higgsing. However, the number of fundamentals gets reduced. We propose the Higgsing condition to be

m_{2r+1}\to\epsilon_{+}+\frac{\tau}{4},\qquad m_{i}\to m_{i},\quad i=1,2,\dots,2r.

(6.201)

Obviously, this Higgsing condition gives the correct modular index in (
6.200
). For the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}(q^{1/4}+q^{3/4})+\chi_{\bf\Lambda^{2}}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{2r+1}e^{m_{i}}\bigg)\Big|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}+\chi_{\bf\Lambda^{2}}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{2r}e^{m_{i}}.

This gives exactly the one-loop function of
\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}
twisted theory.

Now consider the Higgsing from
\mathfrak{su}(2r)^{(2)}\to\mathfrak{su}(2r-1)^{(2)}
. The low energy gauge group reduces from
C_{r}
to
C_{r-1}
.
Let us consider the natural decomposition
C_{r}\to C_{r-1}\times\mathfrak{su}(2)
. It is useful to remark the following representation decompositions

\bf Adj\to(Adj,1)+(F,2)+(1,3),\hskip 11.49994ptF\to(F,1)+(1,2),\hskip 11.49994pt\Lambda^{2}\to(\Lambda^{2},1)+(F,2)+(1,1).

We find the Higgsing can be taken by discretizing the
\mathfrak{su}(2)
into
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
:

m_{\mathfrak{su}(2)}\to\frac{\tau}{4},\quad m_{2r}\to\epsilon_{+}-\frac{\tau}{4},\quad m_{i}\to m_{i},\ i=1,2,\dots,2r-1.

(6.202)

Obviously, this Higgsing condition also gives the correct modular index in (
6.200
). For the one-loop function, we have

\displaystyle\bigg(-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}+\chi_{\bf\Lambda^{2}}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{2r}e^{m_{i}}\bigg)\bigg|_{Higgsing}

\displaystyle\sim-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}(q^{-1/4}+2q^{1/4}+q^{3/4})+\chi_{\bf\Lambda^{2}}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r)}(1+q^{1/2})vq^{-1/4}

\displaystyle+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{2r-1}e^{m_{i}}

\displaystyle=-v\left(\chi_{\bf Adj}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}(q^{1/4}+q^{3/4})+\chi_{\bf\Lambda^{2}}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}q^{1/2}\right)+\chi_{\bf F}^{\mathfrak{sp}(r-1)}(1+q^{1/2})\sum_{i=1}^{2r-1}e^{m_{i}}.

This gives exactly the one-loop function of
\mathfrak{su}(2r-1)^{(2)}
twisted theory.

7
Summary and outlook

In this paper we studied the 2d
(0,4)
SCFTs of BPS strings associated with the twisted circle compactification of all 6d rank-one
(1,0)
SCFTs. The twisted elliptic genera of such 2d theories exhibit many extraordinary properties, which above all are classified by twisted affine Lie algebras. We established the functional equations of the twisted elliptic genera, called twisted elliptic blowup equations, which are the generalization of elliptic blowup equations developed in recent years. This powerful tool enables us to calculate twisted elliptic genera for most rank-one theories from twisted compactification. Although we focused on the one-string case, the recursion formula from twisted elliptic blowup equation allows us to compute the twisted elliptic genera of arbitrary number of strings. We also investigated the modular ansatz of the twisted one-string elliptic genera and found there is naturally a
\Gamma_{1}(N)
modular structure, where
N=2,3,4
is just the twist coefficients of the twisted affine Lie algebras. This is directly connected to the underlying
N
-section Calabi-Yau geometries, which are some genus-one fibered Calabi-Yau threefolds, whose blowup equations after converting to gauge theory language are just the twisted elliptic blowup equations. Our blowup equations can also solve the refined BPS invariants of these Calabi-Yau geometries.

We further studied the generalization of spectral flow symmetry proposed in
DelZotto:2016pvm
;
DelZotto:2018tcj
to the twisted case. We found the spectral flow between R-R and NS-R elliptic genera of one BPS string nicely fits into the twisted situation where the KK charges are naturally shifted by half. In many cases such as the four pure gauge cases, this implies an extra symmetry of the twisted elliptic genera. For theories with 2d localization formulas such as all
\mathfrak{n}=4,\mathfrak{so}(2r+8)^{(2)}
theories, such spectral flow symmetry can be derived explicitly.

In
DelZotto:2016pvm
, Del Zotto and Lockhart conjectured a surprising relation between the one-string elliptic genera of 6d
(1,0)
pure gauge
{G}
SCFTs and the Schur indices of 4d
\mathcal{N}=2

H_{G}
SCFTs for
{G}=\mathfrak{su}(3),\mathfrak{so}(8),F_{4},E_{6,7,8}
. Though still mysterious, this relation has also been shown to exist to some extent for multi-string cases
Gu:2019dan
. It is intriguing to consider whether the twisted elliptic genera of the four pure gauge cases discussed in the current work, i.e.,
\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)},\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(2)},\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)},E_{6}^{(2)}
are related to the Schur indices of some 4d SCFTs, possibly with defects. This relies on certain decompositions of the twisted elliptic genera as infinite copies of certain simpler functions, similar with those in
DelZotto:2016pvm
. Besides, by SCFT/VOA correspondence, the Schur indices of
H_{G}
SCFTs are equivalent to the vacuum characters of VOA
(G)_{-h_{G}^{\vee}/6-1}
. Thus we suspect in the twisted case, the VOA
(G^{(n)})_{-h_{G}^{\vee}/6-1}
will also play a role. Notably, the VOA
(\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)})_{-3/2}
has been recently discussed in
kanade
.

One more fascinating structure of the one-string elliptic genera of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with tensor coefficient
\mathfrak{n}
and gauge group
G
(possibly with matter content) is an elegant decomposition found in
DelZotto:2018tcj
involving the characters of affine Lie algebra
G^{(1)}
at negative level
-\mathfrak{n}
, see the Equations (1.6) and (1.7) there and some recent results in
Duque:2022tub
. Presumably, the twisted elliptic genera we investigated in the current paper should have an analogous decomposition involving the characters of twisted affine Lie algebras at negative levels. We hope to address this issue in the future.

In this paper, we have limited ourselves to the twisted compactification by folding vector multiplets in gauge algebra
G
, which is closely related to twisted affine Lie algebras. As mentioned earlier, it is well-known that there exists another type of twist, that is folding tensor multiplets. This can happen when a higher-rank 6d SCFT exhibits some discrete symmetry in its quiver structure. This type of twisted compactification also gives many interesting 5d KK theories. It should be interesting to study as well the twisted elliptic genera in this setting. In particular, for 6d
(2,0)
SCFTs, this kind of twisted elliptic genera has been studied in
Duan:2021ges
. Besides, one can even consider the twisted elliptic genera associated to the twisted circle compactification of 6d little string theories which has drawn some interests recently
DelZotto:2020sop
;
Bhardwaj:2022ekc
;
DelZotto:2022xrh
. We expect many methods developed in the current work can be extended to the twisted elliptic genera of little string theories.

We can also study 6d SCFTs with defects. There are two types of defects with codimension two and codimension four, which play important roles in the study of the elliptic quantum Seiberg-Witten curves
Chen:2020jla
;
Chen:2021ivd
;
Chen:2021rek
. See
Nazzal:2018brc
;
Razamat:2018zel
;
Nazzal:2021tiu
for other approaches to the elliptic quantum Hamiltonians. The blowup equations for Wilson loops/surfaces and codimension four defect partition functions have been proposed in
Kim:2021gyj
for generic 5d/6d theories on
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times S^{1}
and
\mathbb{R}^{4}\times T^{2}
. It is also interesting to extend the (elliptic) blowup equation to theories with codimension two defects, and study the modular expressions of the (twisted) elliptic genera. See
Nekrasov:2020qcq
;
Jeong:2020uxz
for the blowup equation for codimension two defects in 4d. The solution of the defect partition functions would help to bootstrap new elliptic quantum Seiberg-Witten curves that are corresponding to 6d gauge theories with and without twist.

Appendix A
Useful formulas

We collect some definition formulas used in the twisted elliptic blowup equations (
3.47
). These are the twisted generalizations of those that appeared in
Gu:2020fem
.
When computing the contribution of
\mathring{G}
vector multiplet with KK-charge zero to twisted elliptic blowup equations, we have

\displaystyle A_{{V}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{\beta\in\Delta_{+}}\breve{\theta}_{V}(\beta\cdot m_{\mathring{G}},\beta\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{G}}),

(A.203)

where for
L\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\breve{\theta}_{V}(z,L):=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}m,n\geq 0\\
m+n\leq|L|-1\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}(z+sm\epsilon_{1}+sn\epsilon_{2})}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}m,n\geq 0\\
m+n\leq|L|-2\end{subarray}}\frac{\eta}{\theta_{1}(z+s(m+1)\epsilon_{1}+s(n+1)\epsilon_{2})},

(A.204)

with
s
the sign of
L
. The contributions of vector multiplets with fractional KK-charges can be defined analogously. For
\mathring{G}
representation
R
with non-zero fractional KK-charge
k=1/2,1/3,2/3,1/4,3/4
, we have

\displaystyle A_{{V}}^{frac}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{\beta\in R}\breve{\theta}_{V}^{[k]}(\beta\cdot m_{\mathring{G}},\beta\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{G}}),

(A.205)

where
\breve{\theta}_{V}^{[k]}
is defined by adding characteristic
k
for all
\theta_{1}(\tau,z)
functions as
\theta_{1}^{[k]}(\tau,z)
in (
A.204
). The Jacobi theta functions with characteristics
a
are defined as

\displaystyle\theta_{1}^{[a]}(\tau,z)=

\displaystyle-i\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{k+a}q^{(k+1/2+a)^{2}/2}Q_{z}^{k+1/2+a},

(A.206)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}^{[a]}(\tau,z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{(k+1/2+a)^{2}/2}Q_{z}^{k+1/2+a},

(A.207)

\displaystyle\theta_{3}^{[a]}(\tau,z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{(k+a)^{2}/2}Q_{z}^{k+a},

(A.208)

\displaystyle\theta_{4}^{[a]}(\tau,z)=

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{k+a}q^{(k+a)^{2}/2}Q_{z}^{k+a},

(A.209)

where
Q_{z}=e^{2\pi iz}
. It is important to write every component as genuine Jacobi forms, where the following easy formulas are useful:

\mathrm{PE}\bigg(\Big(Q_{z}+\frac{1}{Q_{z}}\Big)\frac{q^{\frac{1}{2}}}{1-q}\bigg)=\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{24}}\eta(\tau)}{\theta_{4}(\tau,z)},

(A.210)

\mathrm{PE}\bigg(\Big(Q_{z}+\frac{1}{Q_{z}}\Big)\frac{q^{\frac{1}{3}}+q^{\frac{2}{3}}}{1-q}\bigg)=-\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{6}}\eta(\tau)^{2}}{\theta_{1}(\tau,\frac{\tau}{3}+z)\theta_{1}(\tau,\frac{\tau}{3}-z)}=-\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{18}}\eta(\tau)^{2}}{\theta_{4}^{[\frac{5}{6}]}(\tau,z)\theta_{4}^{[\frac{5}{6}]}(\tau,-z)},

(A.211)

\mathrm{PE}\bigg(\Big(Q_{z}+\frac{1}{Q_{z}}\Big)\frac{q^{\frac{1}{4}}+q^{\frac{3}{4}}}{1-q}\bigg)=-\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{12}}\eta(\tau)^{2}}{\theta_{1}(\tau,\frac{\tau}{4}+z)\theta_{1}(\tau,\frac{\tau}{4}-z)}=-\frac{q^{-\frac{1}{48}}\eta(\tau)^{2}}{\theta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}(\tau,z)\theta_{4}^{[\frac{3}{4}]}(\tau,-z)}.

(A.212)

The three formulas are used for the twist coefficient
N=2,3,4
respectively. For example, for
N=2
theories, we can simply define
\breve{\theta}_{V}^{[k]}
by replacing all
\theta_{1}
function to
\theta_{4}
in (
A.204
).

The contribution of hypermultiplets of twisted 6d
(1,0)
theory to the twisted elliptic blowup equations is defined similarly. We have

\displaystyle A^{\mathring{R}}_{{H}}(\tau,m_{\mathring{G}},m_{\mathring{F}},\lambda_{\mathring{G}},\lambda_{\mathring{F}})

\displaystyle=\prod_{\omega\in\mathring{R}^{+}}\breve{\theta}_{H}^{[k^{\prime}]}(w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot m_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot m_{\mathring{F}},w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{F}}).

(A.213)

Here
\mathring{R}^{+}
is half of the total weight space of the twisted matter representations. The
k^{\prime}
is the KK-charge of each component in the twisted matter content. The
k^{\prime}
can be
0,1/2,1/3,2/3,1/4,3/4
. For unity blowup equations, the half weight space can be taken as

\mathring{R}^{+}=\{w_{\mathring{G}}\in R_{\mathring{G}},w_{\mathring{F}}\in R_{\mathring{F}}\,|\,w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{F}}=+{1}/{2}\},

(A.214)

and for vanishing blowup equations as

\mathring{R}^{+}=\{w_{\mathring{G}}\in R_{\mathring{G}},w_{\mathring{F}}\in R_{\mathring{F}}\,|\,w_{\mathring{G}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{G}}+w_{\mathring{F}}\cdot\lambda_{\mathring{F}}>0\}.

(A.215)

The
\breve{\theta}_{H}^{[k^{\prime}]}
functions are defined as

\displaystyle\breve{\theta}^{[k^{\prime}]}_{H}(z,L):=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}m,n\geq 0\\
m+n\leq|L|-3/2\end{subarray}}\frac{\theta_{1}^{[k^{\prime}]}(z+s(m+1/2)\epsilon_{1}+s(n+1/2)\epsilon_{2})}{\eta}\ ,\quad L\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb{Z}.

(A.216)

Appendix B
Modular forms and Jacobi forms

The ring of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
holomorphic modular forms of even weights is generated by the Eisenstein series
E_{4}(\tau)
and
E_{6}(\tau)
.
The
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
weak Jacobi forms of even weights are generated by
E_{4}(\tau)
,
E_{6}(\tau)
and the Eichler-Zagier generators
eichler1985theory
:

\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,\epsilon)=-\frac{\theta_{1}(\tau,\epsilon)^{2}}{\eta(\tau)^{6}}\quad\text{\emph{and}}\quad\phi_{0,1}(\tau,\epsilon)=4\left[\frac{\theta_{2}(\tau,\epsilon)^{2}}{\theta_{2}(\tau)^{2}}+\frac{\theta_{3}(\tau,\epsilon)^{2}}{\theta_{3}(\tau)^{2}}+\frac{\theta_{4}(\tau,\epsilon)^{2}}{\theta_{4}(\tau)^{2}}\right].

(B.217)

These two weak Jacobi forms have index 1 and weight
-2
and
0
respectively. They are frequently used in the modular bootstrap of
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
. We will also use
\phi_{-1,1/2}(\tau,\epsilon):=\phi_{-2,1}(\tau,\epsilon)^{1/2}
for some occasions.

The principal congruence subgroup of level
N
in
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
is defined by

\Gamma(N)=\left\{\left({{}_{a}\ {}_{b}\atop{}^{c}\ {}^{d}}\right)\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\big|\,a\equiv d\equiv 1\!\pmod{N},~b\equiv c\equiv 0\!\pmod{N}\right\}\,.

(B.218)

The Hecke congruence subgroup of level
N
is defined by

\Gamma_{0}(N)=\left\{\left({{}_{a}\ {}_{b}\atop{}^{c}\ {}^{d}}\right)\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\big|\,c\equiv 0\!\pmod{N}\right\}\,.

(B.219)

Another useful congruence subgroup of level
N
is defined by

\Gamma_{1}(N)=\left\{\left({{}_{a}\ {}_{b}\atop{}^{c}\ {}^{d}}\right)\in SL(2,\mathbb{Z})\big|\,a\equiv d\equiv 1\!\pmod{N},~c\equiv 0\!\pmod{N}\right\}\,.

(B.220)

Clearly
\Gamma(N)\subset\Gamma_{1}(N)\subset\Gamma_{0}(N)
. Therefore, there are less modular forms of
\Gamma_{0}(N)
than those of
\Gamma_{1}(N)
.
Define weight-two modular forms of level
N
by

\displaystyle E_{2}^{(N)}(\tau)=-\frac{1}{N-1}\partial_{\tau}\log\left(\frac{\eta(\tau)}{\eta(N\tau)}\right).

(B.221)

Then the ring
M_{*}(N)
of even-weight modular forms for
\Gamma_{0}(N)
,
N\in\{2,3,4\}
is finitely generated by

\displaystyle M_{*}(2)

\displaystyle=\langle E_{2}^{(2)}(\tau),\,E_{4}(2\tau)\rangle,

(B.222)

\displaystyle M_{*}(3)

\displaystyle=\langle E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau),\,E_{4}(3\tau),\,E_{6}(3\tau)\rangle,

(B.223)

\displaystyle M_{*}(4)

\displaystyle=\langle E_{2}^{(2)}(\tau),\,E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau),\,E_{4}(4\tau),\,E_{6}(4\tau)\rangle.

(B.224)

The subscripts are the weights of the modular forms.
Note for
N=3,4
, the generators are not algebraically independent. For example, for
N=3
, it is easy to find

0=E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)^{4}-6E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)^{2}E_{4}(3\tau)-3E_{4}(3\tau)^{2}+8E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)E_{6}(3\tau).

(B.225)

The rings of modular forms for
\Gamma_{1}(N)
usually have more generators. In the case of
N=2
, the two rings are isomorphic. Luckily, for almost all numerators of the modular ansatz in the current work, we only need to use the
\Gamma_{0}(N)
generators. Only in the case of
\mathfrak{n}=1,\mathfrak{so}(8)^{(3)}
, we utilize
\Gamma_{1}(3)
. The ring of
\Gamma_{1}(3)
has four generators:
E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)
,
E_{4}(3\tau)
and two odd ones of weight 3 which are

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(b)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(3\tau)^{9}}{\eta(\tau)^{3}}=q+3q^{2}+9q^{3}+13q^{4}+24q^{5}+27q^{6}+\dots,

(B.226)

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(a)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=E_{1}(\tau)^{3}-18E_{3}^{(b)}(\tau)=1+54q^{2}+72q^{3}+\dots.

where

E_{1}(\tau)=\Theta_{A_{2}}(\tau)=\sum_{n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}q^{n_{1}^{2}-n_{1}n_{2}+n_{2}^{2}}=1+6q+6q^{3}+6q^{4}+\dots.

(B.227)

One can also find some algebraic relations like
E_{2}^{(3)}=E_{1}^{2}
and

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)^{3}-4E_{3}^{(a)}(\tau)^{2}+3E_{4}(3\tau)E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)-72E_{3}^{(a)}(\tau)E_{3}^{(b)}(\tau),

(B.228)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=2E_{3}^{(a)}(\tau)^{2}+E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)^{3}-3E_{4}(3\tau)E_{2}^{(3)}(\tau)-648E_{3}^{(b)}(\tau)^{2}.

For
N=4
, the two generators
E_{2}^{(2)}(\tau),\,E_{2}^{(4)}(\tau)
are sufficient for our purpose.

For
N>1
, we define the weight-
2N
cusp forms of
\Gamma_{1}(N)
by

\displaystyle\Delta_{2N}(\tau)=\frac{1}{q\phi_{-2,1}^{N}(N\tau,\tau)}=q^{N-1}+\mathcal{O}(q^{N}).

(B.229)

For
N=2,3,4
,
\Delta_{2N}(\tau)
are also cusp forms of
\Gamma_{0}(N)
, they take the form from eta functions or the modular generators as

\displaystyle\Delta_{4}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(2\tau)^{16}}{\eta(\tau)^{8}}=\frac{1}{192}\left(E_{4}-(E_{2}^{(2)})^{2}\right),

(B.230)

\displaystyle\Delta_{6}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(3\tau)^{18}}{\eta(\tau)^{6}}=\frac{1}{2^{4}\cdot 3^{6}}\left(7(E_{2}^{(3)})^{3}-5E_{2}^{(3)}E_{4}-2E_{6}\right),

(B.231)

\displaystyle\Delta_{8}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta(2\tau)^{8}\eta(4\tau)^{16}}{\eta(\tau)^{8}}

(B.232)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{17}\cdot 3^{2}\cdot 17}\left(187(E_{2}^{(2)})^{4}-144(E_{2}^{(4)})^{4}-33E_{4}^{2}-E_{6}(154E_{2}^{(2)}-144E_{2}^{(4)})\right).

These
\Delta_{2N}
cusp forms are used in the modular ansatz for the twisted elliptic genera.

Appendix C
More on
\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)^{(2)}
theories

In Section
4.3.3
we have discussed the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of 6d
(1,0)

\mathfrak{su}(2r+1)+2(2r+1)\bf F
theories where we choose the twisted matter content as
(2r+1)(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\mathbf{F}_{1/2})
. Here we choose a different assignment of the KK-charges of the twisted matter content as
(2r+1)(\mathbf{F}_{1/4}+\mathbf{F}_{3/4})
. We notice this choice results in some quite interesting twisted elliptic genera, thus we record our results here. This choice also makes the tensor parameter has integral
B
field.
With this matter content, the circle reduction gives 5d
\mathcal{N}=1
pure
\mathfrak{sp}(r)
gauge theories. Interestingly, we find there exist two possible solutions for the twisted elliptic blowup equations by choosing different characteristics
a
. The two solutions have circle reduction as 5d pure
\mathfrak{sp}(r)_{0}
and
\mathfrak{sp}(r)_{\pi}
theories respectively.
Since the matter content is still invariant under the KK-charge shift by
1/2
, we can expect some spectral flow symmetry.
Most interestingly, we find the two twisted elliptic genera are dual to each other under spectral flow transformation! Besides, both twisted elliptic genera allow perfect modular ansatz. We have fixed the modular ansatz for both elliptic genera for
r=1,2,3
against the results from blowup equations and find perfect agreements. We summarize the relevant data of the modular ansatz in Table
29
. In the following we show some detailed results for
r=1,2
.

\mathfrak{n}=2,\mathfrak{su}(3)^{(2)}

With twisted matter content
3(\mathbf{F}_{1/4}+\mathbf{F}_{3/4})
, we find there are two possible low energy gauge groups
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{0}
or
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
. For each case, we solve the twisted one-string elliptic genus from twisted unity elliptic blowup equations to
q^{4}
order, and collect the Fourier coefficients in
(q,v)
expansion in Tables
30
and
31
. For
\theta=0
in Table
30
, we recognize that the circle reduction of the twisted R-R sector colored orange gives exactly the one
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{0}
instanton Hilbert series, while the circle reduction of the twisted NS-R sector colored red gives exactly the one
\mathfrak{sp}(1)_{\pi}
instanton Hilbert series. For
\theta=\pi
in Table
31
, it is exactly the opposite. It is interesting to see that the two elliptic genera are dual to each other under spectral flow!

Appendix D
Cases with unpaired matter content

We have focused on the twisted compactification of 6d
(1,0)
SCFTs with
paired
matter content in the main context. The unpaired cases are trickier and have been discussed in
Bhardwaj:2020kim
including the Calabi-Yau geometries and 5d low energy limits. The twist in this situation always involves zero mass of some hypermulitplet, thus there should exist no unity elliptic blowup equation or recursion formula for the twisted elliptic genera. However, we notice that sometimes it is possible to pretend the hyper has a free mass for blowup equations and then turn the mass off after solving the twisted elliptic genera. We find that for some twisted theories, this procedure allows consistent solutions of twisted elliptic genera. Remarkably, the solutions have perfect modular ansatz, spectral flow symmetry and required behavior in the
(q,v)
expansion. It is intriguing to consider whether the solutions are indeed physical. We give one example in the following.

Consider the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
twist of
E_{6}+\bf F
theory with
\mathfrak{n}=5
. It was determined in
Bhardwaj:2020kim
that the 5d low energy limit should be a pure
F_{4}
theory. In other words, the twisted matter context should be
\mathring{R}={\bf 1}_{0}+\mathbf{26}_{1/2}
. Let us pretend the fundamental hyper
\mathbf{26}_{1/2}
of
\mathring{G}=F_{4}
has a free mass
m
. This mass can have a shift in blowup equations such that there exist unity twisted elliptic blowup equations. Then from the recursion formula (
3.74
), we compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus to
q
order
5
. Let us denote

\mathbb{E}_{1}(q,v)=q^{-\frac{4}{3}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j}.

(D.233)

We obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
34
. We recognize the leading order of R-R elliptic genus colored orange gives exactly the one-instanton partition function of 5d pure
F_{4}
theory, while the leading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored red gives exactly the one-instanton partition function of 5d
F_{4}+\bf F
theory.

Interestingly, we find that even for twisted matter content

\mathring{R}={\bf 26}_{0}+\mathbf{1}_{1/2}
, elliptic blowup equations still allow perfectly reasonable solution as long as we pretend the hyper has free mass. From the recursion formula (
3.74
), we also compute the twisted one-string elliptic genus of this case to
q
order
5
. Let us denote

\mathbb{E}_{1}(q,v)=q^{-\frac{7}{12}}\sum_{i,j=0}^{\infty}b_{ij}q^{{i}}v^{-2i+j}.

(D.234)

We obtain the coefficients
b_{ij}
in Table
35
.
We recognize the leading order of R-R elliptic genus colored orange gives the one-instanton partition function of 5d
F_{4}+\bf F
theory, while the leading order of NS-R elliptic genus colored red gives the one-instanton partition function of 5d pure
F_{4}
theory. In fact, we find the twisted elliptic genera of the two cases are exactly spectral dual to each other. We also compute the twisted elliptic genera for both matter contents with the mass parameter turned on to
q
order
5
and find the spectral flow relation still holds perfectly. Moreover, we determine the modular ansatz for both cases and find complete consistency with the results from the recursion formula (
3.71
). We have summarized the relevant data of the two modular ansatz in Table
29
.
